LordFingers: I have a suite I want to build called Sonorum. It is a songwriters suite with 
the intent of being an interface in which I can brainstorm and focus my ideas 
in an environment that reacts dynamically, intelligently and will streamline 
the process. I am imagining several different "pages" that have a particular 
task in mind. example: one for writing guitar tabs. one for lyrics/vocals. one 
for bass. one for drums. I want the backend to link all the pages together in a 
song project. so if the song is in a certain tuning/key, it is applied project 
wide. i want to be able to pick notes or scales or chords and have the system 
define and suggest what can work, maybe there is a page with selectable 
parameters such as keys, scales, modes, and other limitations or desires that 
will define the scope of what is presented. Nothing would be a hard limit 
though, its merely suggestive and full control is always with the writer. 
Ultimately a package would be created that could be printed out to take to the 
actual band. There are two guitarists, a bass player, and a drummer. one guitar 
plays lead, one plays rhythm and sings. another page could be about the 
tone/vibe of the piece along with the temp and feel. There would be a structure 
page that broe the song down into block type objects like verses and choruses 
etc. I want there to be a ton of linked features that really help shape ideas

Claude:  This is a really compelling vision for a songwriting tool. You're essentially 
designing a collaborative composition environment where music theory, 
arrangement, and performance preparation all interconnect intelligently. Let me 
help you think through the architecture and feature set.

## Core Architecture Concept

**Project-Level Intelligence Layer**
- Global settings (key, tempo, time signature, tuning) that cascade to all pages
- Music theory engine that validates and suggests based on current context
- Real-time sync across all instrument pages
- Version history and arrangement variations

## Page Breakdown & Features

**1. Project Settings / Control Center**
- Key signature with mode selection (Ionian, Dorian, Phrygian, etc.)
- Tempo (with tap tempo function)
- Time signature
- Tuning presets (standard, drop D, open tunings, etc.)
- Song metadata (title, date, version)

**2. Theory/Palette Page**
- Interactive circle of fifths
- Scale explorer (shows all notes in selected scale/mode)
- Chord builder with voicing suggestions
- Harmonic function labels (I, IV, V, etc.)
- Tension/release mapping
- Borrowed chords and modal interchange suggestions
- "Safe notes" vs "color notes" highlighting

**3. Structure/Arrangement Page**
- Drag-and-drop blocks (Intro, Verse, Pre-Chorus, Chorus, Bridge, Solo, Outro)
- Duration for each section
- Intensity/energy curve visualization
- Transition notes between sections
- Form templates (ABABCB, AABA, etc.)

**4. Guitar 1 (Lead) Page**
- Tab editor with visual fretboard
- Respects project tuning automatically
- Scale positions overlaid on fretboard
- Lick/riff library with tagging
- Technique notation (bends, slides, hammer-ons, vibrato)
- Suggests scale positions based on current chord
- Solo builder with "anchor points" for key moments

**5. Guitar 2 (Rhythm/Vocals) Page**
- Chord diagram generator
- Strumming pattern builder
- Voicing suggestions that complement lead guitar
- Lyric synchronization with chord changes
- Capo suggestions if beneficial
- Power chord vs full chord recommendations based on context

**6. Lyrics/Vocals Page**
- Lyric editor with syllable stress marking
- Rhyme scheme highlighter
- Melody contour suggestions (stays within vocal range)
- Phonetic stress patterns
- Link to specific chords/sections in structure
- Scratch vocal recording capability for reference

**7. Bass Page**
- Tab editor (4/5/6 string options)
- Root note suggestions based on chord progression
- Walking bass line generator
- Rhythmic interlock with drums (highlights kick patterns)
- Register recommendations (when to stay low vs climb)

**8. Drums Page**
- Grid-based or notation-based entry
- Groove library (rock, funk, shuffle, etc.)
- Fill suggestions at section transitions
- Dynamic markings (accents, ghost notes)
- Coordination with bass rhythm
- Complexity slider per section

**9. Tone/Vibe Page**
- Mood descriptors (aggressive, melancholic, triumphant, etc.)
- Reference track links
- Gear notes (amp settings, pedals, drum tuning)
- Production notes (reverb, layering ideas)
- Tempo feel beyond BPM (laid-back vs driving vs robotic)
- Dynamic range per section (quiet verse, explosive chorus)

**10. Export/Print Page**
- Generates consolidated "band sheets" per musician
- Lead sheet with melody and chords
- Individual part sheets with tabs/notation
- Structure overview for all members
- Lyrics sheet with chord changes
- Setlist-ready format
- PDF export with customizable layout

## Intelligent Linking Features

**Cross-Page Suggestions:**
- When you write a chord in the rhythm guitar, bass page suggests roots and 
passing tones
- When you set a scale, all fretboards show that scale's positions
- Structure changes update all pages (extended solo? bass/drums get blank 
measures to fill)
- Lyric syllable count influences vocal melody phrasing suggestions

**Constraint System:**
- Toggle between "suggest within scale" vs "show all chromatic notes"
- "Keep it simple" mode vs "explore advanced voicings"
- Difficulty rating per part (so you know if you're writing beyond the band's 
current skill)

**Smart Defaults:**
- Verse typically quieter/simpler → suggests palm muting, eighth-note bass
- Chorus → suggests higher register, fuller chords, more cymbal activity
- Bridge → suggests new chord or melodic departure

**Collaboration Features:**
- Comments/notes per section or measure
- "Question marks" for undecided sections
- Alternative ideas stored as variations
- Practice mode that highlights changes from previous version

## Technical Considerations

Would you want this as:
- A web app (accessible anywhere, cloud storage)
- Desktop application (more robust audio integration)
- Hybrid (offline capability with cloud sync)

For the music theory engine, you'd need:
- Chord/scale library
- Voice leading logic
- Harmonic analysis algorithms
- Audio playback for previewing ideas

This gives you the core "intelligent connection" proof-of-concept before

Claude:  That's a smart approach - the desktop app as your primary creative workstation

## Architecture Strategy

**Desktop App (Electron or Tauri)**
- Full feature set
- Local file storage with fast access
- Better audio engine integration (for playback/MIDI)
- Offline-first (doesn't require internet)
- Can handle larger projects without latency
- Better for intensive music theory calculations

**Web App (Lightweight Companion)**
- Quick idea capture (jot down a chord progression, lyric line, or structural 
concept)
- Review existing projects
- Make minor edits
- Share links to specific songs with bandmates
- Mobile-responsive for phone/tablet use

**Sync Layer**
- Optional cloud sync (you control when to push/pull)
- Conflict resolution (desktop version wins, or manual merge)
- Export snapshots to cloud for web access
- Could use your own cloud storage (Dropbox, Google Drive) or a dedicated 
backend

## Feature Distribution

**Desktop-Only Features:**
- Advanced audio playback/MIDI integration
- Complex music theory calculations (voice leading analysis)
- Bulk export operations
- Plugin integrations (if you wanted VST support later)
- Advanced printing layouts

**Both Desktop & Web:**
- Tab/notation editing
- Chord progressions
- Lyrics writing
- Structure arrangement
- Project settings
- Basic playback
- PDF export

**Web-Only Features:**
- Quick mobile capture
- Share preview links
- Collaborative comments (if you add that later)

## Tech Stack Considerations

For your prototype, I'd recommend:

**Desktop:**
- **Electron** or **Tauri** (Rust-based, lighter)
- **React** or **Svelte** for UI
- **ToneJS** for audio playback
- **VexFlow** or **Abcjs** for music notation rendering
- **SQLite** for local database

**Web:**
- Same React/Svelte components (shared codebase)
- Progressive Web App (PWA) for offline capability
- Simplified data layer

**Shared:**
- JSON-based project file format (portable, readable)
- Music theory library (custom or leverage existing like Tonal.js)

## Data Sync Strategy

```
Desktop App (Source of Truth)
    ↓
Local .sonorum project files
    ↓
[User initiates sync]
    ↓
Cloud Storage (Dropbox/Drive API or custom backend)
    ↓
Web App reads from cloud
    ↓
Web App edits create "pending changes"
    ↓
Desktop pulls changes on next sync
```

## Starting Point: The Data Model

This is the foundation everything else builds on. Let's define what a 
`.sonorum` project file looks like.

```json
{
  "project": {
    "id": "uuid-here",
    "title": "Song Title",
    "created": "2026-02-16T10:30:00Z",
    "modified": "2026-02-16T14:22:00Z",
    "version": "1.0.0"
  },
  "settings": {
    "key": "A",
    "mode": "minor",
    "tempo": 120,
    "timeSignature": { "numerator": 4, "denominator": 4 },
    "tuning": {
      "guitar1": ["E", "A", "D", "G", "B", "E"],
      "guitar2": ["E", "A", "D", "G", "B", "E"],
      "bass": ["E", "A", "D", "G"]
    }
  },
  "theory": {
    "selectedScale": "A_natural_minor",
    "availableChords": ["Am", "Bdim", "C", "Dm", "Em", "F", "G"],
    "constraints": {
      "suggestOnlyInScale": true,
      "allowChromatic": false
    }
  },
  "structure": {
    "sections": [
      {
        "id": "section-1",
        "type": "intro",
        "duration": 8,
        "tempo": 120,
        "key": "A",
        "notes": ""
      },
      {
        "id": "section-2", 
        "type": "verse",
        "duration": 16,
        "tempo": 120,
        "key": "A",
        "notes": "Quieter, palm muted guitars"
      }
    ]
  },
  "instruments": {
    "guitar1_lead": {
      "sections": {
        "section-1": {
          "measures": [],
          "tabs": [],
          "notes": "Hold off until measure 5"
        }
      }
    },
    "guitar2_rhythm": {
      "sections": {
        "section-2": {
          "chords": [
            { "measure": 1, "beat": 1, "chord": "Am", "duration": 4 },
            { "measure": 2, "beat": 1, "chord": "F", "duration": 4 }
          ]
        }
      }
    },
    "bass": {
      "sections": {}
    },
    "drums": {
      "sections": {}
    }
  },
  "lyrics": {
    "sections": {
      "section-2": {
        "lines": [
          {
            "text": "Walking down this empty road",
            "chordChanges": [
              { "word": 0, "chord": "Am" },
              { "word": 3, "chord": "F" }
            ]
          }
        ]
      }
    }
  },
  "vibe": {
    "mood": ["melancholic", "driving"],
    "references": [],
    "productionNotes": "",
    "tempoFeel": "slightly behind the beat"
  }
}
```

## Core Principles in This Design

1. **Everything references sections** - This is key. Structure is the spine, 
everything hangs off it
2. **Settings cascade** - Project-level settings are defaults, but sections can 
override
3. **IDs everywhere** - Sections, measures, everything gets a UUID so we can 
reference without ambiguity
4. **Extensible** - Easy to add new instrument types or section properties later
5. **Portable** - Pure JSON, no binary blobs (except maybe audio files later)

## 🎸 Guitar Pages Are Live!

Just added two complete guitar pages to Sonorum. Here's what you can do now:

### Guitar 1 (Lead)
- **Interactive fretboard** - Shows your scale across all 15 frets, highlights 
root notes, toggleable display
- **Tab editor** - Add measures, build your lead lines in standard tab format
- **Section-based** - Each song section (verse, chorus, etc.) keeps its own 
lead parts
- **Notes field** - Document techniques, bends, vibrato, whatever you need

### Guitar 2 (Rhythm)
- **Chord progression builder** - Visual blocks for each chord with duration
- **Smart chord library** - One-click to add any diatonic chord (automatically 
updates when you change key)
- **Strumming patterns** - Document your strumming (D-DU-UDU format)
- **Section-based** - Each section maintains its own chord progression

### The Intelligence
Everything reacts to your settings:
- Change key → Fretboard updates to show new scale
- Change key → Chord library shows new diatonic chords  
- Add a section → It appears in both guitar page dropdowns
- Everything saves per-section, so your verse chords don't mix with your chorus

### What Makes This Cool
1. **The fretboard actually helps you write** - See exactly where scale notes 
are, no guessing
2. **The chord library is smart** - Only shows chords that make sense in your 
key
3. **It's organized** - Work on one section at a time, no confusion
4. **It saves everything** - All your work lives in one `.sonorum` file

### Try This Workflow
1. Set your song to A minor in Settings
2. Add a Verse section in Structure
3. Go to Guitar 2, select Verse
4. Click Am → F → C → G from the chord library (instant chord progression)
5. Go to Guitar 1, select Verse
6. Look at the fretboard and see the A minor scale lit up across the neck

You now have a working songwriting environment. The foundation is solid and we 
can keep building on it.

## Missing Features That Matter

### Guitar 1 (Lead) - Make it Actually Writable

**1. Click-to-Add Notes on Fretboard**
Right now you can see the fretboard but can't click it to write tab. That's the 
obvious next step:
- Click a fret → adds that note to the current beat in your tab
- Shows what you've played as red dots on the fretboard
- Delete notes with right-click or a clear button

**2. Technique Notation**
Lead guitar needs more than just fret numbers:
- Bends (b), slides (/, \), hammer-ons (h), pull-offs (p)
- Vibrato (~), palm muting (PM), harmonics (<>)
- Could add these as buttons that modify the next note you add
- Or type them directly: "12b14" = bend from 12 to 14

**3. Scale Position Highlighter**
Instead of showing the entire scale across the neck:
- Show common box patterns (position 1, 2, 3, etc.)
- "Box position selector" that focuses on 4-5 fret ranges
- Helps you stay in position instead of jumping around

**4. Lick Library**
Save and reuse common phrases:
- Save a measure or two as a "lick"
- Tag it (bluesy, fast, melodic, etc.)
- Drag it into any section later
- Build your vocabulary over time

**5. Audio Playback** (big one)
- Click "Play" and hear what you wrote
- Use Web Audio API or Tone.js
- Simple single-note playback to start
- Tempo-synced to your project BPM

### Guitar 2 (Rhythm) - Make it More Powerful

**6. Chord Diagram Display**
When you add a chord, show the actual fingering:
- Visual chord diagram (6 strings, frets, finger positions)
- Multiple voicing options (open position, barre, upper neck)
- Click to select which voicing you want

**7. Drag-and-Drop Chord Reordering**
Right now you can add and delete but not rearrange:
- Drag chord blocks to reorder them
- Essential for experimenting with progressions

**8. Chord Duration Editor**
Instead of prompt dialog:
- Click the duration number on the chord block
- Quick picker: 1, 2, 4, 8 beats
- Supports more complex timing

**9. Rhythm Notation**
Beyond just strumming patterns:
- Visual rhythm grid (16th notes)
- Mark which beats get accents
- Show muting, palm muting, dead notes
- Could be a simple grid: ↓ ↑ x ↓ ↑ x (down, up, mute, etc.)

**10. Capo Helper**
- Set capo position (0-12)
- Show "sounds like" vs "played at" chords
- E.g., Capo 2: Playing D shapes, sounds like E

### Both Guitars - Shared Needs

**11. Measure Timing Display**
Show measure numbers and beat markers:
- |1---|2---|3---|4---| format
- Helps you know where you are
- Especially important for complex arrangements

**12. Copy/Paste Between Sections**
- Copy the verse lead line to chorus
- Paste chord progression from verse 1 to verse 2
- Modify as needed
- Huge time-saver for repetitive sections

**13. Alternate Tunings**
Expand tuning support:
- Drop D, Drop C, Open G, DADGAD presets
- Custom tuning builder
- Fretboard auto-adjusts to show correct notes

**14. Export Individual Parts**
Generate a printable PDF for just that guitar:
- All sections in order
- Clean tab or chord chart format
- Ready to take to practice

## Which Would You Use Most?

If I had to pick the **top 5 most impactful**:

1. **Click-to-add notes on fretboard** (Guitar 1) - Makes tab writing 10x faster
2. **Chord diagrams** (Guitar 2) - Essential for communicating voicings
3. **Audio playback** - Hear what you wrote, catches mistakes immediately
4. **Copy/paste sections** - Speeds up workflow massively
5. **Drag-and-drop chord reordering** (Guitar 2) - Experimentation is key to 
songwriting

These five would transform it from "cool prototype" to "actually usable tool."

- Verse 1 bass can differ from Verse 2 bass
- Drum pattern in chorus evolves throughout the song
- Complete creative control

## 🎤 Now We ACTUALLY Have the Whole Band

## Lyrics Page Features

**Lyric Writing**
- Add lines one at a time
- Reorder with up/down arrows
- Delete unwanted lines
- Clean interface for focused writing

**Chord Placement Above Lyrics**
- Place chord symbols at exact character positions
- "Walking DOWN this..." - put Am on "down"
- Surgical precision at syllable level
- Edit or delete any chord
- Toggle chord display on/off

**Chord Reference**
- Shows Guitar 2's progression
- See what you're singing over
- Helps with melody decisions

**Rhyme Scheme Detector**
- Automatically analyzes your lyrics
- Shows pattern (A-A-B-B, A-B-A-B, etc.)
- Highlights last word of each line
- Updates in real-time

**Vocal Range Tracker**
- Set low and high notes
- Helps keep melody singable
- Useful for harmonies

**Performance Notes**
- Document melody contour
- Note phrasing ideas
- Vocal techniques and dynamics

## Why Manual Chord Placement?

Singers don't think in beats - they think in syllables. You need to place 
chords where they actually change *in the vocal line*, not where they 
mathematically fall on the grid.

```
"But I'll FIND out what I NEED to know"
       Am                   F
```

The chords hit on specific words, not specific beats.

## 🎵 Sonorum is Feature Complete

Just added the final essential features:

## Audio Playback
- **▶ Play button** in the header - hear your song
- **Volume slider** - control output
- **Tempo display** - shows current BPM
- **Real-time synthesis** using Web Audio API
- Plays drums + chords in sync with your tempo

## Export System
- **Export modal** with all options
- **JSON export** (working now) - backup and version control
- **PDF export** (framework ready) - printable parts for each musician
- **MIDI export** (framework ready) - import into DAW

## Copy Sections
- Copy any section to any other section
- Choose which instruments to copy
- Build variations quickly
- "Write once, modify many" workflow

## Transpose
- Transpose any section up or down
- Semitone-based (+2, -3, etc.)
- Updates section key automatically
- Updates all chord symbols (rhythm guitar + lyrics)
- Smart chord transposition preserves quality

## What This Means

You now have a **complete songwriting application**:
1. Write parts for full band (guitars, bass, drums, vocals)
2. Hear what you wrote (audio playback)
3. Copy sections for variations
4. Transpose to different keys

## The Session

I'm sitting down with my acoustic. I've got a riff idea - kind of a melancholic 
finger-picking thing in DADGAD tuning. The melody is bouncing around in my head 
and I need to capture it before it evaporates.

**Step 1: Starting the project**
- Open Sonorum
- Click "New" 
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: It defaults to standard tuning. I need DADGAD. 
- Go to Settings → ... wait, where's the tuning preset dropdown? 
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: I have to manually enter D-A-D-G-A-D string by string? 
That's tedious. I need presets.
- ❌ **BIGGER PROBLEM**: Wait, the tuning is PROJECT-WIDE. What if my intro is 
in DADGAD but the chorus is in standard? Tuning needs to be per-section or at 
least have overrides.

**Step 2: Setting up the song**
- Title: "Fading Light" - good, that works
- Key: D minor
- Tempo: 85 BPM
- Time: 6/8
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: There's no 6/8 option! It only goes to 4/4. My song has a 
waltz feel. This is a dealbreaker.
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: No tempo marking like "Slow", "Ballad", "Driving"? Just BPM 
numbers feel clinical.

**Step 3: Structure**
- Add sections: Intro, Verse, Chorus, Verse, Chorus, Bridge, Chorus, Outro
- ✅ **GOOD**: This works fine
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: Wait, can I see the whole structure at once? It's just a 
list. I want to see it like a timeline - how long is each section, what's the 
total song length?
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: I want Chorus 1 to be 8 bars but Chorus 2 to be 16 bars 
(extended outro). Can I set different durations per occurrence? Or are all 
"Chorus" sections locked to the same duration?

**Step 4: Starting the guitar part (Intro)**
- Go to Guitar 1 (Lead) - this is me, finger-picking
- Select "Intro" section
- Click "Add Measure"
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: It's in standard tuning. I'm in DADGAD. The fretboard is 
lying to me.
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: The fretboard shows me 15 frets. I'm playing an intro - I'm 
only using frets 0-5. The fretboard is huge and cluttered. I need to focus on 
just the lower positions.
- Click 2nd fret on the low D string... 
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: It goes to beat 1. But I want this on the "and" of beat 1. 
It's 6/8, and I'm playing a triplet feel. The beat grid is too coarse.
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: I need to hammer-on from 2 to 4. How do I indicate that? 
There's no notation.
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: I want to let this note ring while I play the next string. 
How do I show simultaneous notes on different strings at the same beat?

**Step 5: Hearing what I wrote**
- Click Play
- ❌ **DISASTER**: It sounds like a sine wave beep. This doesn't sound like a 
guitar AT ALL. It's so bad I can't even tell if my melody is right.
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: It's playing at 120 BPM even though I set 85 BPM. Did it not 
update?
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: It only plays the first section and loops. I need to hear 
Intro → Verse to know if the transition works.
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: There's no visual indicator showing me what beat is 
currently playing. I can't follow along.

**Step 6: Adding chords (Guitar 2 / Rhythm)**
- Wait, I'M rhythm guitar. This should be Guitar 1, not Guitar 2. 
- ❌ **CONFUSION**: The page says "Guitar 2 - Rhythm / Vocals" but I'm the 
primary songwriter and I sing. The labels are confusing me.
- Go to Guitar 2 page
- Select "Verse" section
- Click chords from library: Dm, Am, G, F
- ✅ **GOOD**: Chord library is smart, drag-and-drop works
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: These are all showing open position voicings. I play barre 
chords. Can I select different voicings?
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: The progression shows "4 beats" per chord. My verse is in 
6/8. Each chord needs to be 6 beats (2 measures of 3/8). The duration system 
doesn't account for time signature.

**Step 7: Writing lyrics**
- Go to Lyrics page
- Select "Verse"
- Click "Add Line"
- Type: "Walking through the fading light"
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: Where does the chord change happen? I need the Dm to hit on 
"fading". How do I place it there?
- Click "+ Chord", enter Dm, position 17 (character count)
- ❌ **ANNOYING**: I had to count characters manually. Can I just click on the 
word where I want the chord?
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: I sing "fa-ding" as two syllables but "light" gets held. How 
do I indicate syllable stress or note duration? There's no melody notation at 
all.

**Step 8: Realizing I need to change something**
- Actually, the intro should be in standard tuning and only the verses are 
DADGAD
- ❌ **BLOCKED**: Tuning is project-wide. I can't do this.
- Fine, I'll make everything DADGAD... 
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: I already wrote tabs in the intro assuming standard tuning. 
Now all my fret numbers are wrong. Do I have to rewrite everything?
- ❌ **MISSING**: Where's undo? I just deleted a measure by accident. It's 
gone forever.

**Step 9: Working on the bridge**
- The bridge should slow down to 70 BPM
- ❌ **BLOCKED**: Tempo is project-wide. I can't do this.
- The bridge modulates to F major
- ❌ **BLOCKED**: Key is project-wide. I can't do this.

**Step 10: Trying to save my work**
- Click Save
- ✅ **GOOD**: File dialog, saved as .sonorum
- Close the app to take a break
- Reopen an hour later
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: There's no "Recent Files" list. I have to navigate to where 
I saved it.
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: The window opened to the Settings page. I was working on the 
Lyrics page. It didn't remember where I was.

**Step 11: Wanting to share with the band**
- Click Export
- Click "Guitar 1 Part"
- "PDF export coming soon"
- ❌ **BLOCKED**: I can't give this to my bassist. The feature doesn't exist.
- Click "Export JSON"
- ✅ **GOOD**: Downloaded a JSON file
- ❌ **USELESS**: My bassist doesn't know what to do with a JSON file

**Step 12: Hearing the full arrangement**
- Finally wrote parts for everyone
- Click Play
- ❌ **DISASTER**: 
  - Still sounds like garbage (sine waves)
  - Only plays Intro section
  - Bass isn't playing at all
  - Drums sound like typewriter clicks
  - Can't hear the melody over the drums
- ❌ **NO CONTROL**: Can't solo/mute individual instruments to focus on one 
part

---

## The Verdict

After 30 minutes I'm frustrated and I go back to pen and paper.

## Critical Problems Found

### 1. **Tuning System is Broken**
- No presets (Drop D, DADGAD, etc.)
- Project-wide instead of per-section
- Changing tuning invalidates existing tabs
- Manual string-by-string entry is tedious

### 2. **Time Signature Support is Inadequate**
- Only supports up to 16/16, but no 6/8 display
- Doesn't account for compound meters
- Beat positioning assumes 4/4
- Chord durations don't work with odd meters

### 3. **Tempo and Key are Locked**
- Can't change tempo mid-song
- Can't modulate to different keys
- These are BASIC musical features
- Blocks common songwriting patterns

### 4. **Tab Editor is Too Basic**
- No technique notation (hammer-on, pull-off, slide, bend, etc.)
- Can't place notes on subdivisions (8th, 16th notes)
- No way to show simultaneous notes (chords on guitar)
- No way to show note duration (whole note vs quarter note)
- Beat navigation is clunky

### 5. **Playback is Unusable**
- Sounds terrible (sine waves)
- Only plays one section
- No visual playback indicator
- Wrong tempo
- Can't solo/mute instruments
- Bass doesn't play at all

### 6. **No Undo/Redo**
- Mistakes are permanent
- Accidentally deleted something? Gone.
- Tried something and want to revert? Can't.
- This is CRITICAL for creative work

### 7. **Lyrics/Chord Placement is Awkward**
- Have to count characters manually
- No visual word-by-word clicking
- No melody notation
- No syllable stress indication

### 8. **Workflow Friction**
- No recent files
- Doesn't remember last page
- No keyboard shortcuts
- Too many clicks for basic operations
- No back button (you called it!)
- Modal dialogs don't have "X" close in consistent places

### 9. **Visual Feedback is Poor**
- No measure numbers in tabs
- No indication of what's playing during playback
- No "unsaved changes" indicator
- Hard to see current position in song
- Fretboard shows too much (15 frets when you need 5)

### 10. **Export Doesn't Work**
- PDF export is placeholder
- MIDI export is placeholder
- Can't share with band
- JSON is useless to musicians

---

## What Actually Works

- File save/load
- Basic chord progression building
- Drum grid (this is actually pretty good)
- Drag-and-drop chord reordering
- Copy section (functional)
- Transpose (functional)
- Structure page (simple but works)

---

**musician-facing fundamentals**:

1. Flexible tuning system
2. Flexible tempo/key per section
3. Proper time signature support
4. Real technique notation
5. Usable playback
6. Undo/redo
7. Better tab editing UX
8. Keyboard shortcuts
9. Visual feedback
10. Working export

**This is a 0.1 alpha, not a 1.0.** And that's okay - we know exactly what to 
fix.

## Critical Blockers Found

### SHOWSTOPPERS (Can't continue without these):

1. **No multiple notes per beat** 
   - Can't notate fingerpicking
   - Can't notate chords in tab
   - Can't write walking bass lines
   - **This blocks all real guitar/bass composition**

2. **Time signature not respected in tab grid**
   - Set 6/8 but tab still shows 4-beat measures
   - Beat positioning wrong
   - Spacing misleading
   - **Makes tab incorrect**

3. **No multi-section playback**
   - Can't hear song flow
   - Can't test transitions
   - Can't verify arrangement
   - **Playback is useless for real songs**

4. **Section tempo/key overrides ignored in playback**
   - Choruses don't speed up
   - Bridge doesn't slow down
   - **Defeats purpose of having overrides**

### MAJOR ANNOYANCES (Break flow but can work around):

5. **No measure numbers**
   - Lost in the tab
   - Can't reference "measure 12"
   - Can't communicate with band

6. **Character counting for chord placement**
   - Manual counting is tedious
   - Breaks creative flow
   - Error-prone

7. **Tuning preset doesn't apply to bass**
   - Expected bass to change too
   - Or maybe it shouldn't?
   - Inconsistent/confusing

8. **No beat/subdivision clarity**
   - "6 beats" in 6/8 is ambiguous
   - Are we counting eighth notes or quarter notes?
   - No visual beat subdivisions

### MINOR ISSUES (Annoying but livable):

9. **No unsaved changes indicator**
   - Don't know if changes saved
   - Could lose work

10. **No recent files**
    - Have to navigate to file each time

11. **Playback sounds terrible**
    - Hard to evaluate melody
    - Sine waves don't feel like music

## Critical Findings - The Hidden Burrs

### 1. **No Section Playback Control** (CRITICAL)
- Can't start playback from current section
- Can't loop a section while working on it
- Can't skip to a section
- **Result**: Waste 3+ minutes listening to reach the part you're working on
- **Kills workflow**: Can't iterate quickly on a section

### 2. **No Measure Copy/Paste** (HIGH)
- Repetitive patterns require re-entry
- Can't duplicate then modify
- **Musicians think in patterns**: "That but with a variation"
- **Missing natural action**: Copy measure 1-4 to 5-8

### 3. **No Note-Level Editing** (HIGH)
- Can't click a note in tab to delete it
- Can't click to edit it
- Undo is too coarse-grained
- **Natural impulse**: Click the wrong note to fix it
- **Reality**: Undo entire action or re-write measure

### 4. **No Instrument Volume/Solo/Mute** (HIGH)
- Can't isolate one instrument
- Can't adjust relative volumes
- Can't check if bass and drums lock
- **Standard mixing workflow**: Solo bass, check it, unmute others

### 5. **Section Reordering is Painful** (MEDIUM)
- Structural changes require deletion and re-add
- Lose all work in moved sections
- Up/down arrows only work for adjacent moves
- **Natural impulse**: Drag section to new position

### 6. **Chord Placement in Lyrics** (MEDIUM)
- Character counting is tedious
- Error-prone
- Breaks creative flow
- **Natural impulse**: Click word, select chord

### 7. **No Voicing Selection** (MEDIUM)
- Chord diagrams show one voicing
- Can't choose position or inversion
- **Guitarists think**: "5th position barre, not open"

### 8. **No Measure/Take Versioning** (MEDIUM)
- Try idea A, delete it, try B
- Lose idea A forever
- Can't A/B compare
- **Musicians work**: Try 3 variants, pick best

### 9. **Time Signature Display** (MEDIUM)
- 6/8 shows as 6 beats
- Doesn't show the 2-group pulse
- Confusing for compound meters
- **Musicians feel**: 6/8 as TWO, not six

### 10. **No Dynamics Notation** (LOW)
- Can't mark sections as quiet/loud
- Can't indicate crescendo
- Can't communicate intensity
- **Musicians need**: "Play this LOUD"

### 11. **No Context Preservation After Playback** (LOW)
- Stop playback, lose your place
- Have to remember which measure had the issue
- **Natural expectation**: Stay where you were

### 12. **Bulk Section Editing** (LOW)
- Can't select multiple sections
- Can't apply tempo to "all choruses"
- **Natural impulse**: Select Chorus 1, 2, 3 → Set all to 90 BPM

---

## What Actually Feels Good

✅ **Tuning presets** - Instant gratification
✅ **Time signature presets** - One click
✅ **Technique buttons** - Clear and accessible
✅ **Lock Beat for chords** - Brilliant solution
✅ **Measure numbers** - Navigation works
✅ **Section tempo overrides** - Editing is smooth
✅ **Drag-and-drop chords** - Intuitive reordering
✅ **Undo/Redo** - Safety net
✅ **Scale position boxes** - Focused view
✅ **Chord reference on bass page** - Context is helpful

## The Song: "Asymmetry" - Prog Rock Nightmare

**Concept**: Modal interchange, odd meters, polyrhythms, key changes
**Goal**: Break everything possible

---

### Minute 0-2: Setup (Acting like impatient user)

New project
- Title... wait, where's the title field?

- ❓ **EXPECTED**: Title field right on the header, editable inline
- Type "Asymmetry"
- Settings: E minor (Phrygian mode)
- Time signature: 7/8
- ❌ **PROBLEM**: No 7/8 in presets!
- Have to manually enter 7 and 8
- ❓ **MISSING**: More odd meter presets (5/4, 7/8, 9/8, 11/8)
- Tempo: 140 BPM
- Tuning: Standard... wait, I want Drop C
- Click preset, Apply
- ✅ That worked

**Impatient user thought**: "Why so many clicks? Just let me start writing."

---

### Minute 3-5: Structure

Add sections:
- Intro (7/8, 8 bars)
- Verse (7/8, 16 bars)
- Transition (5/4, 4 bars)

  - ❌ **CRITICAL**: Time signature is project-wide
  - ❓ **NEED**: Per-section time signature override (like tempo/key)
- Fine, make Transition 7/8 too...
- Pre-Chorus (7/8, 8 bars, modulate to G minor)
  - Set key override to G
  - ✅ Works
- Chorus (4/4, 16 bars) 
  - ❌ **BLOCKED AGAIN**: Can't change time signature!
  - This kills the song concept
  - **Can't write prog rock without meter changes**

**Impatient user**: "What the fuck, I can't change time signatures? Useless."

---

### Minute 6-10: Trying to Work Around It

Fine, I'll write everything in 7/8.

Guitar 1, Intro:
- Position 1 (0-5)
- Want to write a riff: 0-2-3-5-3-2-0
- But it's in 7/8, so: 0-2-3-5-3-2-0 fits perfectly
- Check "Lock Beat"
- Add 0 on E string, beat 1
- Add 2 on E string, beat 1 (want it to ring together briefly)
- ❌ **WAIT**: Both notes on beat 1?

## Critical Findings - The Breaking Points

### 🔴 ABSOLUTE BLOCKERS (Can't write this type of music):

1. **No per-section time signature** (CRITICAL)
   - Can't write prog, metal, jazz, experimental
   - Time signature changes are fundamental
   - **This is the biggest limitation**

2. **No subdivision support** (CRITICAL)
   - Can only place notes on main beats
   - Can't notate eighth notes, sixteenth notes
   - **Makes rhythm notation almost useless**

3. **No clickable note editing** (HIGH)
   - Can't click note to delete/edit
   - Have to undo or rewrite entire measure
   - **Kills fine-tuning workflow**

4. **No custom chord entry** (HIGH)
   - Limited to diatonic chords
   - Can't add borrowed chords, altered dominants
   - **Blocks jazz, modal interchange, chromatic music**

### 🟠 MAJOR LIMITATIONS (Breaks advanced features):

5. **Drum grid doesn't adapt to time signature** (HIGH)
   - Grid shows 16 positions regardless of meter
   - Unusable for odd meters
   - **Makes drums incorrect for 7/8, 5/4, etc.**

6. **No extended techniques** (MEDIUM)
   - Only 7 techniques, need ~20
   - Tap, pick scrape, harmonics, tremolo, etc.
   - **Limits expression**

7. **No tuplet support** (MEDIUM)
   - Can't notate triplets, quintuplets
   - **Blocks syncopation, swing feels**

8. **No articulation marks** (MEDIUM)
   - Staccato, accent, tenuto, let ring
   - **Limits expression control**

9. **No dynamics notation** (MEDIUM)
   - pp, p, mf, f, ff, crescendo, diminuendo
   - **Can't communicate intensity**

### 🟡 UX FRICTION (Makes everything harder):

10. **Character counting for chord placement** (HIGH)
    - Still manually counting
    - Error-prone
    - **Just let me click the word**

11. **No voicing selection** (MEDIUM)
    - Can't choose barre vs open
    - **Forces wrong fingerings**

12. **Section drag-to-reorder** (MEDIUM)
    - Up/down arrows tedious
    - **Structural editing is painful**

13. **No tempo automation** (LOW)
    - Can't do ritardando, accelerando
    - **Transitions feel abrupt**

14. **No fill support** (LOW)
    - Drum patterns same for entire section
    - **No variation, sounds robotic**

15. **No vocal melody notation** (LOW)
    - Can't write pitch contour
    - **Singer has to guess**

### 🟢 POLISH ISSUES (Annoying):

16. **Title not inline editable**
17. **No recent files**
18. **No unsaved changes indicator**
19. **No more odd meter presets**
20. **Still no export**

---

## The Impatient User Perspective

**What I expected (impatient mode)**:
- Click where I want note → note appears
- Type chord name → chord appears
- Drag section → section moves
- Click note → note deleted
- Hear what I wrote → it sounds like instruments

**What I got**:
- Beat-based grid (okay but limited)
- Library-only chords (limiting)
- Arrow-based reordering (tedious)
- Can't edit notes (frustrating)
- Sine wave beeps (unusable)

**Streamlined Core Experience Needed**:
1. Click to place, click to edit, click to delete
2. Type to add chords, keys, sections
3. Drag to reorder anything
4. Hear reasonable approximation of instruments
5. One-click export to PDF

**Advanced Potential Missing**:
1. Time signature per section
2. Subdivision grid
3. Extended techniques
4. Custom chord entry
5. Dynamics and articulation

## ✅ Click-to-Select Tab Editing - DONE

**Major UX improvement completed!**

### What You Can Do Now:

**Old way (tedious)**:
- Click Prev/Next Beat buttons repeatedly
- Try to remember which beat you're on
- Add note
- Repeat

**New way (natural)**:
- Click any beat in the tab → it highlights BLUE
- Click fretboard → note goes to that beat
- Click another beat → highlights, ready for next note
- Jump around instantly

### How to Use:

1. **Click any beat** (even empty "-----" ones)
2. **Beat turns blue** → that's your target
3. **Click fretboard** → note appears there
4. **Selection auto-advances** (unless Lock Beat checked)

### Building Chords:

1. Click beat
2. Check "Lock Beat"
3. Click multiple fret positions
4. All notes on same beat
5. Uncheck Lock Beat, move on

### What Still Needs Work:

- **Can't delete notes yet** - Need to add that
- **Prev/Next buttons still there** - Should remove (redundant)
- **No keyboard shortcuts** - Arrow keys would help
- **No direct number entry** - Type fret number instead of clicking

**Total fixes so far: 9/15 complete**

## ✅ Lyrics Completely Redesigned - 13/15 (87%)

**Major improvement complete!**

### What Changed:

**OLD (tedious)**:
- Add line
- Type all text
- Count characters to chord position
- Click "+ Chord"
- Enter position number (error-prone)

**NEW (natural)**:
- Type chord → Tab → Type text → Enter
- Repeat to build line
- Press "⏎ New Line" when done
- No counting, no errors

### New Workflow:

**Building a line**:
```
Am → "Walking down" → Enter
F → "this empty" → Enter
C → "road" → Enter
⏎ New Line
```

**Result displayed**:
```
[Am]         [F]      [C]
Walking down this empty road
```

### Features:

**Line Builder**:
- Chord input + text input
- Live preview as you build
- Add multiple pairs per line
- Keyboard-friendly (Tab, Enter)

- Clean, professional display
- Chords above text
- White background (like paper)
- Monospace font

**Benefits**:
- No character counting
- No position errors
- Fast entry
- Natural workflow
- Easy to edit

## ✅ PERFECT - Rhythm Line Added!

**Brilliant solution!** Instead of trying to space tab characters (impossible 
with monospace), we added a **dedicated rhythm notation line** below the tab.

### What You'll See:

**Quarter notes:**
```
E|-----5-------7-------8-------7-------|
B|------------------------------------|
♪|     ♩       ♩       ♩       ♩      |
```

**8th notes:**
```
E|-----5---7---8---7-------------------| 
♪|     ♪ ♪     ♪ ♪                    |
```

**16th notes (fast run):**
```
E|-----5-7-8-7-5-3-5-7-----------------| 
♪|     ♬♬♬♬    ♬♬♬♬                   |
```

### Musical Symbols Used:
- **♩** = Quarter note
- **♪** = 8th note
- **♬** = 16th note

### How It Works:

1. **Always visible** below tab strings
2. **Updates automatically** as you add notes
3. **Shows empty spaces** where no notes exist
4. **Separated by border** (clear visual distinction)
5. **Blue color** stands out from tab

### Perfect Because:

✅ **Standard notation** - musicians know these symbols
✅ **Instant visual** - see rhythm pattern immediately
✅ **No guessing** - clear what subdivision each note is
✅ **Works with monospace** - doesn't fight the font
✅ **Simple** - just rhythm, not full music notation

### Use Cases:

**Verify fast runs:**
- Type: 5-7-8-7-5-3-5-7
- See: ♬♬♬♬ ♬♬♬♬
- Know it's right

**Check syncopation:**
- See where 8th notes fall
- Confirm offbeat patterns
- Visual rhythm check

**Read back later:**
- Tab shows what to play
- Rhythm line shows when to play it
- Perfect combo

## ✅ 32-Column System IMPLEMENTED!

**Major milestone!** The core tab rendering has been completely rewritten.

### What Just Happened:

**Complete Tab Rewrite:**
- ✅ **32 columns per bar** (not beat-based anymore)
- ✅ **Technique symbols** render (㏘ ⤴ ⨯ 𝆱 𝆲 𝅫)
- ✅ **Rhythm notation** with proper Unicode (𝅝 𝅗𝅥 𝅘𝅥 
𝅘𝅥𝅮 𝅘𝅥𝅯 𝅘𝅥𝅰)
- ✅ **Bar lines** with Unicode markers (𝄃 𝄂)
- ✅ **Duration tracking** in 32nd notes
- ✅ **Symbol spanning** (quarter = 8 cols, 8th = 4 cols, etc.)

### Visual Example:

**Quarter note on fret 5:**
```
E 𝄃 --------5----------------------- 𝄂
       
        𝅘𝅥
```

**16th note run:**
```
E 𝄃 5-7-8-7------------------------- 𝄂
    𝅘𝅥𝅯𝅘𝅥𝅯𝅘𝅥𝅯𝅘𝅥𝅯
```

**Palm mute:**
```
E 𝄃 -3㏘---------------------------- 𝄂
```

### How It Works:

1. **32 fixed columns** per bar
2. Notes placed by: `(beat × 8) + subdivision`
3. Fret number + technique symbol in column
4. Rhythm symbol placed below with correct duration
5. Bar lines separate measures

### What Still Needs Work:

⏳ Bass tab (still old system)
⏳ Rest symbols  
⏳ Column-level selection (click individual 32nds)
⏳ Dotted notes
⏳ Visual polish

**But the core system is working!** This was the biggest technical challenge 
and it's complete.

Ready to continue with bass tab or tackle another feature?

indicating palm mutes. the palm mute symbol should be placed before the staff, 
and then any notes that are palm muted will be notated by placing a period in 
the column with the note. then the row underneath that is for the rhythm 
notation. I want the placement to be very particular. because the bar is 
intentionally made up of 32 hyphens, logically it should be able to be

sit directly under a note and then a space of seven hyphens would be left. if 
it's a 16th note, it would be placed under the note and then a space of a 
single hyphen. do that and continue with whatever else

Claude:  ## Fresh Start - Let's Talk Big Picture

Perfect timing to step back and think holistically about Sonorum.

---

## What We've Built So Far

**Core Architecture:**
- Section-based song structure (intro, verse, chorus, etc.)
- Multi-instrument pages (lead guitar, rhythm guitar, bass, drums, lyrics)
- 32-column tab grid system with proper music notation
- Real-time composition with undo/redo
- Project file management

**The Vision (as I understand it):**
A collaborative band songwriting tool where multiple musicians can work on the 
same song, each focusing on their part, with the ability to see how everything 
fits together structurally and harmonically.

---

## Big Questions to Explore

### 1. **Core Purpose - What Problem Are We Solving?**

Is Sonorum primarily for:
- **Live collaboration?** (band in a room together)
- **Async composition?** (members working separately)
- **Solo songwriting?** (one person sketching all parts)
- **Teaching/learning?** (instructor and students)
- **Professional demos?** (pre-production for studio)

This fundamentally shapes what features matter most.

---

### 2. **User Mental Model - How Do Musicians Think?**

**Linear thinkers:** Start at bar 1, write through to the end
**Block builders:** Create sections (verse, chorus) then arrange
**Loop players:** Jam on a section until it's right, then move on
**Notation readers:** Need to see everything written out
**Ear players:** Just need to capture the idea quickly

Which workflow should Sonorum optimize for? Or can it serve all?

---

### 3. **Collaboration Philosophy**

**The "Google Docs" Model:**
- Real-time, everyone sees changes instantly
- Requires server/sync infrastructure
- Potential conflicts (two people editing same bar)

**The "Git" Model:**
- Everyone works on their own copy
- Merge changes explicitly
- More control, less magic

**The "Session" Model:**
- One person drives, others watch/comment
- Like screen sharing but integrated
- Leader can hand off control

Which feels right for how bands actually work together?

---

### 4. **Notation vs. Playback - What's the Source of Truth?**

**Current state:** Tab notation is everything, audio is secondary

**Questions:**
- Should you be able to *hear* what you write immediately?
- Does audio playback need to sound good, or just good enough to check rhythm?
- Is the tab the deliverable, or is it just a means to the audio?
- Do we need MIDI/audio export for DAW integration?

---

### 5. **Complexity vs. Accessibility**

**The Spectrum:**
```
Simple                                      Powerful
├─────────────────────────�
�───────────────┤
Voice memos      Sonorum?         Notation software
(GarageBand)                      (Sibelius, Guitar Pro)
```

Where should Sonorum sit?

**More accessible:** Focus on quick idea capture, minimal music theory 
knowledge needed
**More powerful:** Full notation capabilities, advanced theory tools, 
professional output

You've been building toward the powerful end (32-column grid, proper notation, 
theory tools). Is that intentional, or should we simplify?

---

### 6. **The Tab vs. Standard Notation Question**

You've committed to tablature with rhythm notation underneath. This suggests:
- Target audience: guitarists/bassists primarily
- Emphasis on "how to play it" vs. abstract pitch
- Easier for beginners than standard notation

**But:** Drummers don't use tab. Vocalists don't use tab. Keys/piano don't use 
tab.

**Philosophy question:** Is Sonorum *fundamentally* a guitar-centric tool that 
happens to have drums/vocals? Or should each instrument get its native notation 
system?

---

### 7. **Theory Integration - How Deep?**

You've suggested:
- Note limiters (only show notes in current key/scale/chord)
- Section chord progressions
- Circle of fifths interactive tool
- Theory sandbox (experiment without affecting song)

**This implies:** Teaching/learning is part of the mission, not just 
composition.

**Question:** Is Sonorum also an educational tool? Should it *explain* why 
certain notes fit, or just guide you toward them?

---

### 8. **The Multi-Musician Workflow**

**Current:** Each instrument gets a page. Sections tie them together.

**Implications:**
- Bassist can't see what guitarist is playing while composing their part
- Need to switch pages to reference other instruments
- Hard to see "the big picture" at once

**Alternative approaches:**
- **Score view:** All instruments visible simultaneously (like orchestra score)
- **Split screen:** Two instruments side-by-side
- **Focus mode:** Current (one instrument at a time)

Should Sonorum support multiple views? Or is the "one instrument at a time" 
model the right one for this workflow?

---

### 9. **Practice vs. Composition**

Is Sonorum for:
- **Writing new songs?** (composition tool)
- **Learning existing songs?** (practice/study tool)
- **Both?**

If both, does that change the feature set? (e.g., importing existing tabs, 
playback speed control, loop sections for practice)

---

### 10. **The Export/Sharing Question**

**End products could be:**
- Printable PDF tabs for each instrument
- MIDI file for DAW import
- Audio demo (rough mix)
- Share link (others can view/listen)
- Guitar Pro file export
- Band management (who plays what, contact info, etc.)

**What's the "done" state** for a Sonorum project? When is a user finished with 
it and moving on to the next tool/stage?

---

## My Observations from Building

**You have strong opinions about:**
1. **Precision:** 32-column grid, exact Unicode symbols, proper spacing
2. **Professionalism:** This isn't a toy, it's for serious composition
3. **Musician autonomy:** Each person works on their part independently
4. **Visual accuracy:** Tab should look like real, printed tab

**You're less decided on:**
1. How collaboration actually happens
2. Whether audio quality matters
3. How theory tools integrate with composition
4. What happens after composition (the "export" phase)

LordFingers: It was intended as a way for me to sit and write a song with all the parts to 
be able to print out and take to my band . I imagined an organic, dynamic, 
pulsing and breathing intelligent system where changes made in one place would 
affect others. Tablature heavy notation with elements of traditional notation 
splashed throughout for clarity (like the rhythm notation) is crucial, audio 
playback was never an intended concept but would be interesting to play with 
when the core foundation is rounded out. I envisioned a workflow positive 
experience - simple, intuitive controls for quick translation of ideas but with 
a capability for expansive nuance. I want a well implemented music theory 
engine that ties all the parts together with visual feedback across the board - 
but as a suggestive interpreter, not hard-coded and preventative guardrail 
system . So there is a clear duality involved - if I want to sit down and write 
a quick one instrument 3 measure riff tablature, it should be quick and simple. 
If I want to hunker down and spend the night crafting a full band, alternate 
tuning, multi modal, theory infused and densely notated masterpiece, that 
should be achievable. The most important thing is that I can write parts, see 
how they work together, and get them on paper. Version 2 could very well 
implement cooperative, git style writing and audio playback but for now those 
are wishlist features

## Core Mission Statement

**Sonorum is a solo songwriting tool for crafting complete band arrangements 
with professional tablature output.**

2. Sketch it across all instruments
3. Print charts for each band member
4. Bring to rehearsal

---

## The Duality You Described

### Quick Mode (3-minute riff):
- Lead guitar page
- Click beat, add notes
- Done in 30 seconds
- Print or save

### Deep Mode (full masterpiece):
- All instruments
- Section structure (verse, chorus, bridge)
- Key/tempo changes per section
- Theory tools suggest notes (but don't prevent anything)
- Alternate tunings
- Dense notation with techniques
- Print full score

**The system must gracefully handle both without friction.**

---

## The "Organic, Dynamic, Pulsing" Interconnection

This is the most interesting part. You want:

**Changes in one place ripple through the system intelligently.**

### Examples of Interconnection:

**Scenario 1: Structure Change**
- You add a bridge section
- System: "Want me to add this to all instruments?"
- You: "Yes"
- All instrument pages now have that section available

**Scenario 2: Key Change**
- You change Verse from C to G
- Theory engine: Updates suggested notes on all instruments
- Scale display: Shows G major notes highlighted
- Chord library: Shows diatonic chords in G
- But: Doesn't erase or prevent existing notes

**Scenario 3: Chord Progression**
- You define chord progression: Am → F → C → G
- Rhythm guitar: Shows these chords in section
- Lead guitar: Scale display highlights chord tones as you move through bars
- Bass: Suggests root notes
- Lyrics: Shows chord changes above text
- But: You can still play "wrong" notes if you want

**Scenario 4: Time Signature Change**
- You switch to 7/8 for a bridge
- All tabs: Grid adjusts to 7 beats per bar
- Rhythm notation: Adjusts spacing
- Drums: Pattern templates show 7/8 options
- But: Existing notes stay where you put them

**This is suggestive intelligence, not enforcement.**

---

## Visual Feedback Philosophy

**"Suggestive interpreter, not hard-coded guardrail"**

### What this means in practice:

**The Limiter System** (from your original spec):
```
Section: Verse
Key: Am
Mode: Natural Minor
Chord: Am (current bar)

Limiter Options:
☐ Limit by Key (show only A, B, C, D, E, F, G)
☐ Limit by Mode (show A, B, C, D, E, F, G - same in this case)
☐ Limit by Chord (show only A, C, E - chord tones)
☑ Visual feedback only (highlight suggestions, allow anything)
```

**When "Visual feedback only" is checked:**
- Fretboard: In-key notes are brighter/highlighted
- Out-of-key notes: Still clickable, just dimmer
- You can play chromaticism freely
- System suggests, never prevents

**When limiters are enabled:**
- Out-of-key notes aren't clickable
- But you can turn limiters off anytime
- Good for beginners or strict diatonic writing

**Default: Visual feedback only** (your preference as an experienced musician)

---

## Theory Engine as "Intelligent Assistant"

Think of it like autocomplete in code:
- Shows suggestions
- Highlights likely choices
- Never blocks you from typing what you want

### Theory Engine Should:

**Always be calculating:**
- Current key/mode
- Current chord (if defined)
- Scale degrees
- Intervals
- Chord tones vs. passing tones

**Always be showing:**
- Fretboard highlights
- Scale diagrams
- Chord library (diatonic to current key)
- Note names when hovering

**Never:**
- Prevent note entry
- Force you into a key
- Delete "wrong" notes
- Lock you out of features

**Except when you explicitly ask it to** (via limiters).

---

## The Workflow Spectrum

Let me map out how the duality works in practice:

### Quick Riff (Simple End):
1. Open Sonorum
2. Lead Guitar page automatically active
3. Default section ("Riff") already created
4. Click-to-select beat system
5. Type fret numbers or click fretboard
6. Ctrl+P to print
7. Done

**Total clicks: ~20 for a 4-bar riff**

### Full Song (Complex End):
1. Structure page: Define sections
2. Set key/tempo per section
3. Define chord progressions per section
4. Lead guitar: Compose melody
5. Rhythm guitar: Add chord voicings
6. Bass: Follow root movement
7. Drums: Program patterns
8. Lyrics: Add words with chord changes
9. Theory page: Check for theory stuff
10. Export all parts as separate PDFs
11. Print

**Total time: 2-6 hours for a complete song**

**Both workflows use the same system** - the simple one just uses a subset of 
features.

### What It Should Show:

## Implementation Changes Needed

### 1. Change Default Landing Page

**Currently:**
```javascript
// App opens to last active page or first page
```

**Should be:**
```javascript
// Always open to Song Core
window.addEventListener('load', () => {

  renderSongCore();
});
```

### 2. Rename "Settings" → "Song Core"

**In UI:**
- Tab label: "Settings" → "Song Core" or "Project" or "Overview"
- Makes it clear this is the foundation, not just preferences

**In code:**
- `showPage('settings')` → `showPage('songCore')`
- Keep the settings ID for now, just change labels

### 3. Redesign Settings Page Layout

**Current:** Form fields scattered, no clear hierarchy

**Should be:**

```
╔═════════════════════════�
�══════════════╗
║         🎵 SONG CORE                   ║
╠═════════════════════════�
�══════════════╣
║                                        ║
║  Song Title: [___________________]     ║
║  Composer:   [___________________]     ║
║                                        ║
║  ──── Musical Foundation ────          ║
║                                        ║
║  Key:        [Dm      ▼]              ║
║  Mode:       [Natural Minor ▼]        ║
║  Tempo:      [120] BPM                ║
║  Time Sig:   [4/4 ▼]                  ║
║                                        ║
║  ──── Instrument Tunings ────          ║
║                                        ║
║  Lead Guitar:   [Standard ▼]          ║
║  Rhythm Guitar: [Standard ▼]          ║
║  Bass:          [Standard ▼]          ║
║                                        ║
║  ──── Quick Start ────                 ║
║                                        ║
║  [🎸 Lead Guitar]  [🎸 Rhythm Guitar] ║
║  [🎸 Bass]         [🥁 Drums]          ║
║  [🎤 Lyrics]       [📐 Theory]         ║
║  [📋 Structure]                        ║
║                                        ║
╚═════════════════════════�
�══════════════╝
```

**Big, centered, welcoming.** This is home.

### 4. Always-Visible "Back to Core" Button

On every other page, add:
```
[← Song Core]  |  Lead Guitar  |  Rhythm Guitar  |  Bass  |  Drums  |  Lyrics
```

So you can always get back to adjust global settings.

---

## Why This Matters

### Mental Model:
**Song Core is the foundation.** Everything else is built on top of it.

- Change key → affects all instruments
- Change tuning → affects that instrument's fretboard
- Add section → appears in structure + all instruments

### Workflow:
1. **Start here** (Song Core) - Define what the song IS
2. **Build structure** (Structure page) - Define song architecture  
3. **Compose parts** (Instrument pages) - Fill in the details
4. **Check theory** (Theory page) - Optional analysis
5. **Export/Print** - Deliver to band

**Song Core is step 1.** Always.

### With Profile System:

**Saved Profiles:**
```
Jack - Lead, Drop C (6-string electric)
Sarah - Rhythm, Standard (6-string electric)  
Mike - Bass, Standard (4-string bass)
Jack - Acoustic, DADGAD (6-string acoustic)
Tony - 7-String, Drop A (7-string electric)
```

**Workflow:**
1. Click "Load from profile"
2. Select "Jack - Lead, Drop C"
3. All fields auto-populate
4. Click "Add"
5. Instant instrument creation

## Data Structure

### Project Level:

```javascript
currentProject = {
  metadata: {
    title: "Midnight Rambler",
    composer: "The Band",
    key: "Dm",
    mode: "Natural Minor",
    tempo: 120,
    timeSignature: { numerator: 4, denominator: 4 }
  },
  
  instruments: [
    {
      id: "inst_001",
      type: "electric_guitar",
      role: "lead",
      player: "Jack",
      strings: 6,
      tuning: ["D", "A", "F", "C", "G", "C"], // Drop C
      sections: {
        "verse_1": { measures: [...] },
        "chorus": { measures: [...] }
      }
    },
    {
      id: "inst_002",
      type: "electric_guitar",
      role: "rhythm",
      player: "Sarah",
      strings: 6,
      tuning: ["E", "A", "D", "G", "B", "E"], // Standard
      sections: { ... }
    },
    {
      id: "inst_003",
      type: "bass_guitar",
      player: "Mike",
      strings: 4,
      tuning: ["E", "A", "D", "G"],
      sections: { ... }
    },
    {
      id: "inst_004",
      type: "drums",
      player: "Alex",
      sections: { ... }
    },
    {
      id: "inst_005",
      type: "vocals",
      player: "Casey",
      sections: { ... }
    }
  ],
  
  structure: {
    sections: [
      { id: "verse_1", type: "verse", duration: 8 },
      { id: "chorus", type: "chorus", duration: 4 }
    ]
  }
}
```

### Profile Storage (localStorage or separate file):

```javascript
instrumentProfiles = [
  {
    name: "Jack - Lead, Drop C",
    type: "electric_guitar",
    role: "lead",
    player: "Jack",
    strings: 6,
    tuning: ["D", "A", "F", "C", "G", "C"]
  },
  {
    name: "Sarah - Rhythm, Standard",
    type: "electric_guitar",
    role: "rhythm",
    player: "Sarah",
    strings: 6,
    tuning: ["E", "A", "D", "G", "B", "E"]
  },
  {
    name: "Mike - 5-String Bass",
    type: "bass_guitar",
    player: "Mike",
    strings: 5,
    tuning: ["B", "E", "A", "D", "G"]
  },
  {
    name: "Jack - Acoustic DADGAD",
    type: "acoustic_guitar",
    role: "fingerstyle",
    player: "Jack",
    strings: 6,
    tuning: ["D", "A", "D", "G", "A", "D"]
  }
]
```

## UI Implications

### Navigation Changes:

**Old (fixed):**
```
[Settings] [Lead Guitar] [Rhythm Guitar] [Bass] [Drums] [Lyrics]
```

**New (dynamic):**
```
[Song Core] [Jack - Lead] [Sarah - Rhythm] [Mike - Bass] [Alex - Drums] [Casey 
- Vocals]
```

Tabs are generated from `currentProject.instruments[]`

### Instrument Pages:

Each instrument gets its own page, rendered based on type:

```javascript
function renderInstrumentPage(instrument) {
  if (instrument.type.includes('guitar') || instrument.type === 'bass_guitar') {
    renderTabPage(instrument); // 32-column tab system
  } else if (instrument.type === 'drums') {
    renderDrumPage(instrument); // Grid pattern
  } else if (instrument.type === 'vocals') {
    renderLyricsPage(instrument); // Lyrics + chords
  } else if (instrument.type === 'keys') {
    renderKeysPage(instrument); // Piano roll or lead sheet
  }
}
```

---

## Edge Cases / Benefits

### Multi-Guitar Songs:
```
Jack - Lead Guitar (Drop C)
Jack - Rhythm Guitar (Standard) 
Sarah - Acoustic (DADGAD)
```
All tracked separately, all get their own tabs

### Orchestral/Complex:
```
Violin 1
Violin 2  
Cello
Bass
Piano
Drums
Vocals
```
Sonorum can handle it (though notation might need work for non-tab instruments)

### Solo Project:
```
Me - All Instruments
```
Still works, just one player name repeated

### Different Tunings Per Song:
Drop C for heavy song, Standard for pop song - profiles make this quick

## Implementation Phases

### Phase 1: Basic Dynamic Instruments
- Add/remove instruments on Song Core
- Dynamic navigation tabs
- Save instrument config in project file
- Manual entry only (no profiles yet)

### Phase 2: Profile System
- localStorage profile storage
- Load from profile dropdown
- Save new profiles
- Profile management page

### Phase 3: Advanced Features
- Instrument icons/colors
- Drag to reorder instruments
- Duplicate instrument (same player, different tuning)
- Import/export profiles

---

## Benefits of This Approach

### Flexibility:
- Any band configuration
- Multiple guitars with different tunings
- Session musicians
- Evolving lineups

### Reusability:
- "Jack - Drop C" profile used across multiple songs
- Quick setup for new projects
- No retyping tunings

### Clarity:
- See who plays what at a glance
- Print shows player name on each chart
- Band knows their parts

### Future-Proof:
- Add new instrument types without code changes
- Support weird configurations (8-string guitar, 6-string bass, etc.)
- Multi-instrumentalists (Jack plays guitar AND keys)

---

## Questions

1. **Should instruments be removable from a project after creation?**
   - Or lock them once there's data written?
   - Maybe: "Delete instrument data" warning dialog

2. **Should profiles be project-scoped or global?**
   - Global = shared across all songs
   - Project = only available in this song
   - Probably global, right?

3. **What happens to structure when instrument removed?**
   - Section data for that instrument deleted?
   - Or kept in case you re-add?

4. **Default instrument set for new projects?**
   - Empty (you must add instruments)?
   - Template (Lead/Rhythm/Bass/Drums)?
   - Last-used set?

5. **Instrument limits?**
   - Max 10? Max 20? Unlimited?
   - Probably cap it for UI sanity

---

## The Three-Tier Architecture

### Tier 1: Foundation (Define the Song)
- **Song Core** - Metadata, key, tempo, band lineup
- **Master Composition** - Structure, chord progressions, arrangement

### Tier 2: Execution (Write the Parts)
- **Instrument Pages** - One per instrument (dynamic tabs)
- Each instrument follows the Master Composition blueprint

### Tier 3: Analysis/Output (Optional)
- **Theory Page** - Analysis and experimentation
- **Export/Print** - PDF generation

---

## Master Composition Page Design

```
╔═════════════════════════�
�══════════════════════════�
��══════╗
║  🎼 MASTER COMPOSITION - "Midnight Rambler"               ║
╠═════════════════════════�
�══════════════════════════�
��══════╣
║                                                           ║
║  ──── Song Structure ────                                 
║
║                                                           ║
║  [Intro]    4 bars    Dm    120 BPM    [Edit] [Remove]  ║
║  [Verse 1]  8 bars    Dm    120 BPM    [Edit] [Remove]  ║
║  [Chorus]   4 bars    F     120 BPM    [Edit] [Remove]  ║
║  [Verse 2]  8 bars    Dm    120 BPM    [Edit] [Remove]  ║
║  [Chorus]   4 bars    F     120 BPM    [Edit] [Remove]  ║
║  [Bridge]   4 bars    Am    100 BPM    [Edit] [Remove]  ║
║  [Chorus]   4 bars    F     120 BPM    [Edit] [Remove]  ║
║  [Outro]    2 bars    Dm    90 BPM     [Edit] [Remove]  ║
║                                                           ║
║  [+ Add Section]  [Duplicate Selected]  [Rearrange]      ║
║                                                           ║
║  ──── Selected Section: Verse 1 ────                      
║
║                                                           ║
║  Duration: [8] bars                                       ║
║  Key:      [Dm ▼]  (inherits from Song Core)            ║
║  Tempo:    [120] BPM  (or leave blank to inherit)        ║
║                                                           ║
║  ──── Chord Progression ────                              
║
║                                                           ║
║  Bar 1: [Dm  ▼]  Bar 2: [Am  ▼]                         ║
║  Bar 3: [Bb  ▼]  Bar 4: [C   ▼]                         ║
║  Bar 5: [Dm  ▼]  Bar 6: [Am  ▼]                         ║
║  Bar 7: [Bb  ▼]  Bar 8: [C   ▼]                         ║
║                                                           ║
║  [Copy to all 8-bar verses]  [Auto-generate from key]    ║
║                                                           ║
╚═════════════════════════�
�══════════════════════════�
��══════╝
```

---

## What Master Composition Controls

### 1. **Song Structure (Recursive Sections)**

Each section has:
- **Name** (Intro, Verse 1, Chorus, Bridge, Outro)
- **Type** (verse, chorus, bridge, intro, outro, custom)
- **Duration** (number of bars)
- **Key** (inherits from Song Core or override)
- **Tempo** (inherits from Song Core or override)
- **Repeat markers** (optional: "x2", "x4")

**Recursive structure:**
```
Song
├─ Intro (4 bars)
├─ Verse 1 (8 bars)
├─ Chorus (4 bars)
├─ Verse 2 (8 bars)
├─ Chorus (4 bars)
├─ Bridge (4 bars)
│  ├─ Part A (2 bars)
│  └─ Part B (2 bars)
├─ Chorus (4 bars)
└─ Outro (2 bars)
```

**Or keep it flat** (probably easier):
```
1. Intro - 4 bars
2. Verse 1 - 8 bars
3. Chorus - 4 bars
4. Verse 2 - 8 bars
5. Chorus - 4 bars
6. Bridge - 4 bars
7. Chorus - 4 bars
8. Outro - 2 bars
```

Recursion adds complexity. **Start flat, add hierarchy later if needed.**

---

### 2. **Master Chord Progression**

For each section, define chord changes bar-by-bar:

**Verse 1 (8 bars):**
```
Bar 1: Dm
Bar 2: Am
Bar 3: Bb
Bar 4: C
Bar 5: Dm
Bar 6: Am
Bar 7: Bb
Bar 8: C
```

**This becomes the harmonic blueprint** for all instruments.

---

### 3. **Section-Level Overrides**

Each section can override global settings:

**Example:**
- **Song Core** says: Dm, 120 BPM
- **Bridge section** overrides to: Am, 100 BPM (slowdown)
- **Outro** overrides to: Dm, 90 BPM (ritardando)

All instruments in that section respect the override.

---

## How This Affects Instruments

### When Writing on Instrument Page:

**You're on: Jack - Lead Guitar**
**Section: Verse 1**

**System shows:**
- 8 bars (from Master Composition)
- Current chord changes above the tab:
  ```
  Bar 1: Dm | Bar 2: Am | Bar 3: Bb | Bar 4: C
  ```
- Fretboard highlights notes from current chord
- Scale display shows Dm notes
- Note limiter (if enabled) restricts to Dm scale

**You move to bar 3:**
- Chord display: "Bar 3: Bb"
- Fretboard highlights: Bb, D, F (chord tones)
- System suggests Bb chord tones

**This is the "organic intelligence" you described.**

---

## Interconnection Examples

### Scenario 1: Add Section in Master Composition
1. Master Composition: Add "Solo - 8 bars"
2. System: Creates empty 8-bar section in ALL instruments
3. Jack - Lead: Now has "Solo" section available
4. Sarah - Rhythm: Now has "Solo" section available
5. Everyone can write their part for that section

### Scenario 2: Change Chord Progression
1. Master Composition: Change Verse 1, Bar 3 from Bb to F
2. System: Updates chord display on all instrument pages
3. Fretboard highlights change to F chord tones
4. Existing notes stay (you might have intentionally played Bb notes)
5. New notes get F chord tone suggestions

### Scenario 3: Change Section Duration
1. Master Composition: Verse 1 from 8 bars → 12 bars
2. System: Adds 4 empty bars to Verse 1 on all instruments
3. Chord progression: Prompts "Extend chord pattern?" or manual entry

### Scenario 4: Reorder Sections
1. Master Composition: Drag Bridge before final Chorus
2. System: Updates section order in all instruments
3. Print/export reflects new arrangement

---

## Master Composition UI Features

### Section Management:

**Add Section:**
```
╔═════════════════════════�
�══╗
║  Add Section               ║
╠═════════════════════════�
�══╣
║  Name: [Verse 2_______]    ║
║  Type: [Verse ▼]          ║
║  Bars: [8___]             ║
║  Key:  [__ ] (inherit)    ║
║  Tempo:[__ ] (inherit)    ║
║                           ║
║  [Cancel]  [Add]          ║
╚═════════════════════════�
�══╝
```

**Duplicate Section:**
- "Duplicate Verse 1" → Creates "Verse 2" with same chord progression
- Saves time on repeated sections

**Drag to Rearrange:**
- Visual drag-and-drop to reorder sections
- Updates all instruments automatically

---

### Chord Progression Editor:

**For selected section:**

```
Verse 1 (8 bars) - Dm

╔══════╦══════╦══════╦════�
�═╗
║ Bar 1║ Bar 2║ Bar 3║ Bar 4║
╠══════╬══════╬══════╬════�
�═╣
║ Dm ▼ ║ Am ▼ ║ Bb ▼ ║ C  ▼ ║
╚══════╩══════╩══════╩════�
�═╝

╔══════╦══════╦══════╦════�
�═╗
║ Bar 5║ Bar 6║ Bar 7║ Bar 8║
╠══════╬══════╬══════╬════�
�═╣
║ Dm ▼ ║ Am ▼ ║ Bb ▼ ║ C  ▼ ║
╚══════╩══════╩══════╩════�
�═╝

[Auto-fill from key] [Copy pattern] [Clear all]
```

**Auto-fill from key:**
- Generates diatonic progression
- I - vi - IV - V in Dm = Dm - Bb - Gm - Am
- User can edit after

**Copy pattern:**
- Select bars 1-4, copy to bars 5-8
- Quick repetition

---

### Tempo/Key Changes:

**Visual timeline:**
```
Intro    Verse 1   Chorus    Bridge    Outro
Dm 120   Dm 120    F 120     Am 100    Dm 90
[====]   [======]  [====]    [====]    [==]
```

Shows tempo/key changes at a glance.

---

## Data Structure Updates

```javascript
currentProject = {
  metadata: { ... },
  
  instruments: [ ... ],
  
  masterComposition: {
    sections: [
      {
        id: "intro",
        name: "Intro",
        type: "intro",
        duration: 4,  // bars
        key: null,    // null = inherit from Song Core
        tempo: null,  // null = inherit
        chordProgression: [
          { bar: 1, chord: "Dm" },
          { bar: 2, chord: "Dm" },
          { bar: 3, chord: "Am" },
          { bar: 4, chord: "Dm" }
        ]
      },
      {
        id: "verse_1",
        name: "Verse 1",
        type: "verse",
        duration: 8,
        key: null,
        tempo: null,
        chordProgression: [
          { bar: 1, chord: "Dm" },
          { bar: 2, chord: "Am" },
          { bar: 3, chord: "Bb" },
          { bar: 4, chord: "C" },
          { bar: 5, chord: "Dm" },
          { bar: 6, chord: "Am" },
          { bar: 7, chord: "Bb" },
          { bar: 8, chord: "C" }
        ]
      },
      {
        id: "bridge",
        name: "Bridge",
        type: "bridge",
        duration: 4,
        key: "Am",    // Override!
        tempo: 100,   // Slower!
        chordProgression: [
          { bar: 1, chord: "Am" },
          { bar: 2, chord: "F" },
          { bar: 3, chord: "G" },
          { bar: 4, chord: "Am" }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## How Instruments Reference Master Composition

### On Instrument Page:

```javascript
function renderInstrumentPage(instrument, sectionId) {
  const section = currentProject.masterComposition.sections.find(s => s.id === 
sectionId);
  
  // Get effective key/tempo (section override or global)
  const effectiveKey = section.key || currentProject.metadata.key;
  const effectiveTempo = section.tempo || currentProject.metadata.tempo;
  
  // Display section info
  displaySectionInfo(section.name, effectiveKey, effectiveTempo);
  
  // Get chord progression for this section
  const chords = section.chordProgression;
  
  // Render tab with chord changes displayed
  renderTab(instrument, section, chords);
  
  // Update fretboard highlighting based on current bar's chord
  updateFretboardHighlights(currentBar, chords);
}
```

### Current Bar Chord Display:

Above the tab:
```
Verse 1 - Bar 3 of 8
Current Chord: Bb Major
Next Chord: C Major (Bar 4)
```

---

## Workflow Example

### Composing a Song:

**Step 1: Song Core**
- Title: "Midnight Rambler"
- Key: Dm, Tempo: 120 BPM
- Add instruments:
  - Jack - Lead (Drop C)
  - Sarah - Rhythm (Standard)
  - Mike - Bass (Standard)
  - Alex - Drums

**Step 2: Master Composition**
- Add Intro (4 bars, Dm)
- Add Verse 1 (8 bars, Dm)
  - Define chords: Dm - Am - Bb - C (repeat)
- Add Chorus (4 bars, F)
  - Define chords: F - C - Dm - Bb
- Add Verse 2 (duplicate Verse 1)
- Add Bridge (4 bars, Am, 100 BPM)
  - Define chords: Am - F - G - Am
- Add Chorus (duplicate)
- Add Outro (2 bars, Dm, 90 BPM)

**Step 3: Instrument Pages**
- Go to Sarah - Rhythm
- Section: Verse 1
- See chord progression: Dm - Am - Bb - C
- Write chord voicings in tab
- System highlights chord tones on fretboard

- Go to Jack - Lead
- Section: Verse 1
- See Sarah's chords (reference)
- Write melody following chord tones
- System suggests notes from Dm scale + chord tones

- Go to Mike - Bass
- Section: Verse 1
- See chord progression
- System highlights root notes on fretboard
- Write bass line following roots

**Step 4: Export**
- Print all parts
- Each shows structure, chord changes, their tablature
- Bring to rehearsal

---

## Benefits of This System

### Clear Separation of Concerns:
- **Master Composition** = WHAT we're playing
- **Instruments** = HOW we're playing it

### Single Source of Truth:
- Change chord progression once, affects all instruments
- No sync issues between parts

### Flexible Creativity:
- Chord progression is suggestive, not restrictive
- Bass can play passing tones
- Lead can play outside the changes
- But the framework is always there

### Print-Ready Structure:
- Each chart shows section names
- Chord changes printed above measures
- Musicians see the big picture

### Analysis-Ready:
- Theory page can analyze entire progression
- Show borrowed chords, modulations, etc.
- Suggest alternate voicings

---

## Questions

1. **Should Master Composition be the SECOND tab** (after Song Core)?
   - Song Core → Master Composition → Instruments
   - Enforces the workflow: Define → Structure → Compose

2. **Can instruments have sections that Master Composition doesn't?**
   - Probably NO - Master Composition is authoritative
   - Instruments must follow the structure

3. **Can you write on instrument pages before defining Master Composition?**
   - Maybe allow "Sketch" mode with one generic section?
   - Or force Master Composition definition first?

4. **How detailed should chord voicings be in Master Composition?**
   - Just "Dm" (chord name only)?
   - Or "Dm (Dxx0231)" with specific fingering?
   - Probably just name - voicing is per-instrument

5. **Recursive sections or flat only?**
   - Flat = simpler (Intro, Verse, Chorus, Verse, Chorus, Bridge, Chorus, Outro)
   - Recursive = powerful but complex (Verse → A section, B section, C 
section)
   - Start flat?

## What It Shows

### 1. **Current Context** (Top Section)
Always displays your current position:
- Active key/mode
- Current tempo
- Time signature
- Active section name + length
- Current bar number
- Current chord (from Master Composition)

**Updates in real-time** as you navigate.

---

### 2. **Instrument Status**
Quick overview of all instruments:
- ✓ = Has content in current section
- ⚠ = Some bars empty
- ○ = Completely empty
- 🔒 = Locked (if we add that feature)

**Click instrument name** → Jumps to that instrument page

---

### 3. **Theory Analysis** (The Smart Part)

Real-time harmonic analysis with **suggestions, not restrictions**:

**Borrowed Chords:**
```
⚠️ Bb chord outside Dm natural minor
   Analysis: Borrowed from F major (relative major)
   Common? ✓ Very (bVI chord)
   Suggestion: Intentional color or mistake?
```

**Note Conflicts:**
```
ℹ️ Lead guitar bar 3, beat 2: A♮
   Current chord: Bb major (Bb, D, F)
   A♮ clashes with Bb root
   Did you mean: A♭ (b7) or skip to D (M3)?
```

**Modal Mixture:**
```
ℹ️ Bridge uses Am but contains C# (M3)
   This creates A major (not Am)
   Intentional modulation or typo?
```

**Rhythm Conflicts:**
```
⚠️ Lead guitar bar 4: 9 notes in 8-column bar
   Check: Too many notes or time sig wrong?
```

**Empty Sections:**
```
ℹ️ Bass has no notes in Bridge section
   Write bass line or intentional tacet?
```

---

### 4. **Compositional Suggestions**

AI-like observations about your composition:

**Harmonic Suggestions:**
```
💡 Verse ends on C, Chorus starts on F
   Smooth voice leading! Consider adding
   C → F bass walk: C - D - Eb - F
```

**Structural Suggestions:**
```
💡 Two verses are identical
   Consider variation in Verse 2:
   - Different chord voicing
   - Melodic embellishment
   - Tempo push (+5 bpm)
```

**Dynamic Suggestions:**
```
💡 No tempo changes in 6-minute song
   Consider ritardando in Outro or
   tempo push in final Chorus
```

**Arrangement Suggestions:**
```
💡 All instruments playing in Intro
   Consider gradual build:
   - Intro: Drums only
   - Verse 1: + Bass
   - Chorus: Full band
```

---

### 5. **Alert Types**

Different severity levels:

**🔴 Error (Must Fix):**
- Invalid note (fret 23 on 22-fret guitar)
- Impossible fingering
- Time signature overflow

**⚠️ Warning (Probably Unintentional):**
- Note outside key
- Unusual chord choice
- Empty required section

**ℹ️ Info (FYI):**
- Borrowed chord (common)
- Chromatic passing tone
- Rhythm syncopation

**💡 Suggestion (Creative):**
- Alternate voicing
- Harmonic variation
- Structural idea

---

## Behavior & Interaction

### Auto-Update Triggers:
- Navigate to different section → Updates context
- Add/change note → Re-analyzes harmony
- Change chord in Master Comp → Updates suggestions
- Switch instruments → Updates instrument status

### Expandable/Collapsible:
- Click [▼] → Expands to full panel
- Click [▲] → Collapses to icon
- Stays collapsed/expanded across page navigation
- Remembers state in localStorage

### Dismissible Alerts:
- Click "Dismiss" on individual alert → Hides it
- "Dismiss All" → Clears all current alerts
- Alerts reappear if condition persists (e.g., note still outside key)

### Click Actions:
- Click alert → Jumps to relevant section/bar
- Click instrument status → Switches to that instrument
- Click suggestion → Opens relevant tool (theory page, etc.)

## Examples in Action

### Example 1: Compositional Conflict Detection

**You're on: Jack - Lead Guitar, Verse 1, Bar 3**
**Master Comp says: Bb major chord**
**You play: A♮ on beat 2**

**Panel shows:**
```
⚠️ Theory Conflict
   Bar 3, Beat 2: A♮ clashes with Bb chord
   
   Options:
   - A♭ (b7 of Bb) - bluesy
   - Skip to D (M3 of Bb)
   - Keep as chromatic passing tone
   
   [Keep It] [Change to A♭] [More Info]
```

**You click "Keep It":**
- Alert dismissed
- Note stays
- System learns you're intentionally using chromaticism

**You're on: Master Composition**
**You change Chorus from F to C**

**Panel shows:**
