```
ℹ️ Key Change Detected
   Chorus: F → C (up a 5th)
   
   Affected:
   - Sarah (Rhythm): 12 notes in F chord voicings
   - Mike (Bass): 4 F root notes
   
   Suggestions:
   💡 Update Sarah's voicings to C?
   💡 Transpose Mike's bass line?
   
   [Auto-Update] [Keep Original] [Review]
```

**You click "Review":**
- Shows side-by-side comparison
- You can manually adjust or auto-transpose

**You're on: Song Core**
**Bridge section exists but Mike (Bass) hasn't written anything**

```
⚠️ Incomplete Sections
   
   Bridge (4 bars):
   ✓ Jack (Lead) - complete
   ✓ Sarah (Rhythm) - complete
   ⚠ Mike (Bass) - empty
   ○ Alex (Drums) - empty
   
   [Go to Mike - Bass] [Mark as Tacet]
```

**"Mark as Tacet"** = Intentionally silent, don't warn again

---

### Example 4: Borrowed Chord Education

**You're on: Sarah - Rhythm, Verse 1**
**You use Bb chord in Dm song**

**Panel shows:**
```
ℹ️ Borrowed Chord Detected
   Bb major in Dm context
   
   Analysis:
   - Bb is bVI in Dm
   - Borrowed from F major (relative)
   - Very common in rock/pop
   - Creates darker, modal sound
   
   Famous examples:
   - "Stairway to Heaven" (Am → G/B → C → D/F# → F → Am)
   - "All Along the Watchtower"
   
   [Learn More] [Dismiss]
```

**This is teaching + composing simultaneously.**

## Technical Implementation

### Data Structure:

```javascript
songIntelligence = {
  context: {
    key: "Dm",
    mode: "Natural Minor",
    tempo: 120,
    timeSignature: "4/4",
    currentSection: "verse_1",
    currentBar: 3,
    currentChord: "Bb"
  },
  
  instrumentStatus: [
    { id: "inst_001", name: "Jack (Lead)", status: "complete", bars: 8 },
    { id: "inst_002", name: "Sarah (Rhythm)", status: "complete", bars: 8 },
    { id: "inst_003", name: "Mike (Bass)", status: "empty", bars: 0 },
    { id: "inst_004", name: "Alex (Drums)", status: "empty", bars: 0 }
  ],
  
  alerts: [
    {
      id: "alert_001",
      type: "warning",
      category: "theory",
      message: "Bb chord outside Dm natural minor",
      details: "Borrowed from F major (relative major). Common bVI chord.",
      location: { section: "verse_1", bar: 3 },
      dismissed: false
    },
    {
      id: "alert_002",
      type: "info",
      category: "harmony",
      message: "Lead guitar: A♮ on beat 2 conflicts with Bb chord",
      details: "A♮ clashes with Bb root. Consider A♭ (b7) or D (M3).",
      location: { section: "verse_1", bar: 3, beat: 2, instrument: "inst_001" },
      dismissed: false
    }
  ],
  
  suggestions: [
    {
      id: "sugg_001",
      type: "suggestion",
      category: "harmony",
      message: "Bridge at Am could use C major for brighter feel",
      details: "Current progression: Am - F - G - Am is dark. Try Am - F - C - 
G for lift.",
      dismissed: false
    }
  ]
}
```

---

### Real-Time Analysis Engine:

```javascript
function analyzeComposition() {
  const alerts = [];
  const suggestions = [];
  
  // 1. Check for borrowed chords
  const borrowedChords = detectBorrowedChords(
    currentProject.masterComposition,
    currentProject.metadata.key
  );
  borrowedChords.forEach(bc => {
    alerts.push({
      type: "info",
      category: "theory",
      message: `${bc.chord} borrowed from ${bc.source}`,
      details: bc.explanation
    });
  });
  
  // 2. Check for note conflicts
  currentProject.instruments.forEach(instrument => {
    const conflicts = detectNoteConflicts(
      instrument,
      currentProject.masterComposition
    );
    conflicts.forEach(c => {
      alerts.push({
        type: "warning",
        category: "harmony",
        message: c.message,
        details: c.suggestion
      });
    });
  });
  
  // 3. Check for empty sections
  const emptyInstruments = detectEmptySections(
    currentProject.instruments,
    currentProject.masterComposition.sections
  );
  emptyInstruments.forEach(ei => {
    alerts.push({
      type: "warning",
      category: "completeness",
      message: `${ei.name} has no notes in ${ei.section}`
    });
  });
  
  // 4. Suggest variations
  suggestions.push(...generateSuggestions(currentProject));
  
  return { alerts, suggestions };
}
```

---

### Smart Suggestions:

```javascript
function generateSuggestions(project) {
  const suggestions = [];
  
  // Detect repetitive sections
  const sections = project.masterComposition.sections;
  const duplicates = findDuplicateSections(sections);
  if (duplicates.length > 0) {
    suggestions.push({
      message: `${duplicates.length} sections are identical`,
      details: "Consider variation in repeated sections for interest"
    });
  }
  
  // Detect static tempos
  const tempoChanges = sections.filter(s => s.tempo !== null).length;
  if (tempoChanges === 0 && sections.length > 4) {
    suggestions.push({
      message: "No tempo changes in multi-section song",
      details: "Consider ritardando in outro or tempo push in final chorus"
    });
  }
  
  // Detect harmonic opportunities
  const unusedChords = detectUnusedDiatonicChords(project);
  if (unusedChords.length > 0) {
    suggestions.push({
      message: `Unused diatonic chords: ${unusedChords.join(', ')}`,
      details: "Might add color to progression"
    });
  }
  
  return suggestions;
}
```

## Questions

1. **Should alerts be per-section or song-wide?**
   - Show only alerts for current section?
   - Or all alerts across entire song?
   - Toggle between views?

2. **How aggressive should suggestions be?**
   - Minimal (only obvious issues)?
   - Moderate (common improvements)?
   - Chatty (lots of creative ideas)?

3. **Should it remember dismissed alerts?**
   - Permanently (never show again)?
   - Per-session (show next time you open)?
   - Smart (show again if context changes)?

4. **Position: Always top-right?**
   - Or draggable anywhere?
   - Or dockable (left/right/bottom)?

5. **Mobile/small screen behavior?**
   - Hide entirely?
   - Bottom sheet?
   - Hamburger menu?

## Questions You Might Have

**Q: Should I use a bundler (webpack, vite)?**
A: Not yet. ES6 modules work fine for development. Add bundler later for 
production.

**Q: Do I need a local server?**
A: Yes. ES6 modules require HTTP protocol.
```bash
python3 -m http.server 8000
# Then open http://localhost:8000
```

**Q: What about CSS?**
A: Start with a single `main.css`. Split later if it gets big.

**Q: Should I write tests as I go?**
A: Optional. Theory engine would benefit from tests. UI can wait.

---

## If You Get Stuck

### Common Issues:

**"Module not found"**
- Check file extension (.js)
- Check relative path (./utils not utils)
- Check case sensitivity (helpers.js not Helpers.js)

**"State not updating"**
- Make sure you're calling the update function
- Check browser console for errors
- Verify store is initialized

**"Nothing renders"**
- Check main.js is loaded (type="module")
- Check console for syntax errors
- Start with simple HTML string before complex logic

**Cons:**

Claude:  ## Complete Stack Explanation - From Scratch

Let me break down **every concept**, assuming zero knowledge. Think of this as 
your crash course.

---

## **Part 1: What Even Is a "Stack"?**

A **development stack** = the collection of tools and languages you use to 
build your app.

**Analogy:** Building a house
- **Language** (JavaScript) = Your building materials (wood, metal)
- **Framework** (React, Vue) = Pre-fab wall kits vs. cutting your own lumber
- **Build Tools** (Vite, Webpack) = Power tools that make construction faster
- **Dev Server** = The construction site where you work

---

## **Part 2: The Core Language - JavaScript**

### What is JavaScript?
The **only** programming language that runs in web browsers. It makes web pages 
interactive.

**Without JavaScript:**
```html
<h1>Song Title</h1>
```
Just static text. Boring.

**With JavaScript:**
```javascript
const title = document.querySelector('h1');
title.addEventListener('click', () => {
  title.textContent = 'New Song Title!';
});
```
Now clicking changes the title. Interactive!

### What are ES6 Modules?
A **modern way** to organize JavaScript into separate files.

**Old way (avoid this):**
```html
<script src="file1.js"></script>
<script src="file2.js"></script>
<script src="file3.js"></script>
```
Everything in global scope. Files can conflict. Order matters. Messy.

**Modern way (ES6 modules):**
```javascript
// constants.js
export const NOTES = ['C', 'D', 'E'];

// main.js
import { NOTES } from './constants.js';
console.log(NOTES); // ['C', 'D', 'E']
```

**Why better?**
- **Isolated**: Each file has its own scope
- **Explicit**: You see exactly what each file needs
- **Organized**: Easy to understand dependencies
- **Modern**: Industry standard since 2015

**This is what we're using for Sonorum.**

---

## **Part 3: Vanilla JS vs. Frameworks**

### What is "Vanilla JS"?
Plain JavaScript with no framework. You write everything from scratch.

**Example - Vanilla JS:**
```javascript
function renderButton() {
  const button = document.createElement('button');
  button.textContent = 'Click me';
  button.onclick = () => alert('Clicked!');
  document.body.appendChild(button);
}
```

**You're in control of everything.** More code, but you understand it all.

---

### What is a Framework? (React, Vue, Svelte)

A **pre-built system** that handles common tasks for you.

**Example - React:**
```javascript
function Button() {
  return <button onClick={() => alert('Clicked!')}>Click me</button>;
}
```

**Framework handles:**
- Creating the button element
- Attaching the event listener
- Updating the page when things change

**Trade-off:**
- ✅ Less code to write
- ✅ Built-in patterns
- ❌ Learning curve (you must learn React's way)
- ❌ Extra complexity
- ❌ Larger file sizes

---

### **My Choice for Sonorum: Start Vanilla**

**Why?**

1. **You control everything** - No "magic" happening behind the scenes
2. **Learn fundamentals** - Understanding how browsers work
3. **No overhead** - Don't learn a framework you might not need
4. **Easy to upgrade later** - Can add React later if you want

**Example for Sonorum:**
```javascript
// Vanilla approach
export function renderSongCore(project) {
  const html = `
    <div class="song-core">
      <h1>${project.title}</h1>
      <input value="${project.tempo}" />
    </div>
  `;
  document.getElementById('app').innerHTML = html;
}
```

Simple. Clear. No framework needed.

---

## **Part 4: Development Servers**

### Why Do You Need a Server?

**The problem:**
```
file:///Users/you/sonorum-v2/index.html
```

Opening HTML files directly (file://) **breaks ES6 modules** for security 
reasons. Browsers won't load them.

**The solution:** Serve files over HTTP (http://localhost)

---

### Option 1: Python's Built-In Server (Recommended)

**What is it?**
Python includes a simple web server. One command starts it.

**Command:**
```bash
python3 -m http.server 8000
```

**What this does:**
- Serves all files in current directory
- Runs on port 8000
- Access at: http://localhost:8000

**Why good?**
- ✅ Already installed (comes with Python)
- ✅ Zero setup
- ✅ One command
- ✅ Perfect for development

**Downside:**
- ❌ Manual refresh (F5 to see changes)
- ❌ No "hot reload" (page doesn't auto-update)

---

### Option 2: Node.js `serve` Package

**What is Node.js?**
JavaScript runtime that runs outside the browser. Lets you use JavaScript for 
servers and tools.

**Command:**
```bash
npx serve
```

**What is npx?**
Runs Node packages without installing them globally. Think "run this tool once."

**Why good?**
- ✅ Simple
- ✅ Slightly prettier output than Python

**Downside:**
- ❌ Requires Node.js installed
- ❌ Still no hot reload

---

### Option 3: VS Code Live Server Extension

**What is it?**
A VS Code extension that adds a "Go Live" button.

**Install:**
1. Open VS Code
2. Click Extensions (left sidebar)
3. Search "Live Server"
4. Install

**Usage:**
Right-click `index.html` → "Open with Live Server"

**Why good?**
- ✅ One-click start
- ✅ Auto-refresh on file save
- ✅ No command line needed

**Downside:**
- ❌ Requires VS Code

---

### **My Choice: Start with Python**

**Tonight:**
```bash
cd sonorum-v2
python3 -m http.server 8000
```

Then open http://localhost:8000

**Why?**
- Already installed
- Simple
- Works perfectly for initial development

**Later:** Add Vite for hot reload (explained below)

---

## **Part 5: Build Tools - Vite Explained**

### What is a Build Tool?

A program that:
1. **Bundles** your code (combines many files into one)
2. **Minifies** (removes spaces, shortens names)
3. **Transpiles** (converts modern JS to older JS for compatibility)
4. **Optimizes** (makes files smaller/faster)

**Without build tool:**
```
index.html loads:
  - main.js
  - constants.js
  - helpers.js
  - theory.js
  - ...25 more files

= 28 separate HTTP requests
= Slower page load
```

**With build tool:**
```
index.html loads:
  - bundle.min.js (all code combined & compressed)

= 1 HTTP request
= Faster page load
```

---

### What is Vite?

A **modern build tool** specifically designed for development speed.

**What Vite does:**

**During Development:**
1. **Hot Module Reload** - Change a file → Page updates instantly (no F5)
2. **Fast startup** - Starts in milliseconds
3. **ES6 modules** - No bundling needed in dev

**For Production:**
4. **Bundle** - Combines files
5. **Minify** - Compresses code
6. **Optimize** - Tree-shaking (removes unused code)

---

### Vite vs. Webpack (Older Tool)

**Webpack:**
- Been around since 2012
- Complex configuration
- Slow to start (bundles everything even in dev)
- Industry standard but dated

**Vite:**
- Created 2020 by Vue.js author
- Zero config for most projects
- Instant start (uses native ES modules)
- Modern standard

**Analogy:**
- Webpack = Desktop tower PC (powerful but slow to boot)
- Vite = MacBook (instant-on, modern)

---

### **My Choice: Add Vite Later**

**Why not start with Vite?**
- You're learning. Simpler = better.
- Python server works fine for now.
- Vite adds a `node_modules` folder with 400+ files. Overwhelming.

**When to add Vite?**
- When manual refresh gets annoying
- When you have 10+ modules working
- When you want production builds

**How to add later:**
```bash
npm init -y              # Create package.json
npm install -D vite      # Install Vite
npm run dev              # Start Vite dev server
```

Takes 2 minutes to add when you're ready.

---

## **Part 6: Package Managers - npm Explained**

### What is npm?

**npm** = Node Package Manager

**What it does:**
Installs code libraries other people wrote so you don't reinvent the wheel.

**Example:**
You want to generate PDFs. Instead of writing a PDF generator from scratch 
(thousands of lines), you:

```bash
npm install jspdf
```

Now you have a PDF library:
```javascript
import jsPDF from 'jspdf';
const doc = new jsPDF();
doc.text('Hello', 10, 10);
doc.save('song.pdf');
```

**Where does it come from?**
npmjs.com - A registry of 2+ million packages.

---

### What is package.json?

A file that lists your project's dependencies.

**Example:**
```json
{
  "name": "sonorum",
  "version": "2.0.0",
  "dependencies": {
    "jspdf": "^2.5.0"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "vite": "^5.0.0"
  }
}
```

**Why important?**
- Share project → Others run `npm install` → They get exact same packages
- Like a recipe card for your project's ingredients

---

### What is node_modules?

A folder where npm installs packages.

**Warning:** This folder gets HUGE (50-200 MB, thousands of files).

**Rule:** Add to `.gitignore` (don't commit to Git)

---

### **My Choice: Skip npm Initially**

**Tonight:** No packages needed. Pure JavaScript.

**Later:** When you need PDF export or other features:
```bash
npm init -y
npm install jspdf
```

---

## **Part 7: Git - Version Control**

### What is Git?

A **time machine** for code. Save checkpoints as you work.

**Without Git:**
```
sonorum-v2-backup.zip
sonorum-v2-backup-final.zip
sonorum-v2-backup-final-REALLY-FINAL.zip
```
Messy. Can't see what changed. Nightmare.

**With Git:**
```bash
git commit -m "Add song core page"
git commit -m "Add tab renderer"
git commit -m "Add PDF export"
```

**Benefits:**
1. **History** - See what changed and when
2. **Undo** - Go back to any previous version
3. **Branches** - Try experimental features safely
4. **Backup** - Push to GitHub for safety

---

### Basic Git Workflow

```bash
# One-time setup
cd sonorum-v2
git init

# After each feature
git add .
git commit -m "Description of what you did"

# Undo changes
git checkout -- filename.js

# See history
git log --oneline
```

---

### **My Choice: Use Git from Day 1**

**Why?**
- Free insurance policy
- Commit after each working feature
- Never lose work
- Industry standard skill

**How to start:**
```bash
cd sonorum-v2
git init
git add .
git commit -m "Initial project structure"
```

Done. Now commit after each session.

---

## **Part 8: Code Editor - VS Code**

### What is VS Code?

Microsoft's free code editor. Industry standard.

**Why not just Notepad?**
- Syntax highlighting (colors make code readable)
- Auto-complete (suggests as you type)
- Error detection (red squiggles under mistakes)
- Extensions (add superpowers)

**Download:** code.visualstudio.com

---

### Essential Extensions

**ESLint** - Catches code errors before you run it
```javascript
const x = 5
const x = 10  // ESLint: "x is already declared"
```

**Prettier** - Auto-formats code on save
```javascript
// You type:
function  add(  a,b   ){return a+b}

// Prettier formats to:
function add(a, b) {
  return a + b;
}
```

**Live Server** - One-click server start (alternative to Python)

---

### **My Choice: VS Code with ESLint + Prettier**

**Setup (one time):**
1. Install VS Code
2. Install ESLint extension
3. Install Prettier extension
4. Create `.vscode/settings.json`:
```json
{
  "editor.formatOnSave": true,
  "editor.defaultFormatter": "esbenp.prettier-vscode"
}
```

Now code auto-formats every time you save. Beautiful.

---

## **Part 9: CSS - Styling**

### What is CSS?

**CSS** = Cascading Style Sheets. Makes things look pretty.

**HTML (structure):**
```html
<button>Click me</button>
```

**CSS (style):**
```css
button {
  background: blue;
  color: white;
  padding: 10px;
  border-radius: 5px;
}
```

Result: Blue button with white text and rounded corners.

---

### CSS Preprocessors (Sass, Less) - DO YOU NEED THEM?

**What are they?**
Languages that compile to CSS. Add features like variables, nesting.

**Sass example:**
```scss
$primary: blue;

.button {
  background: $primary;
  
  &:hover {
    background: darken($primary, 10%);
  }
}
```

Compiles to regular CSS.

**My opinion:** **Not needed anymore.**

Modern CSS has:
- **Variables:** `--primary: blue;`
- **Nesting:** Coming in CSS Nesting (or just write more selectors)
- **Functions:** `calc()`, `clamp()`, etc.

**My choice:** Plain CSS with CSS Custom Properties (variables).

---

### CSS Frameworks (Tailwind, Bootstrap)

**What are they?**
Pre-made CSS classes you can use.

**Tailwind example:**
```html
<button class="bg-blue-500 text-white px-4 py-2 rounded">
  Click me
</button>
```

No need to write CSS. Just add class names.

**Pros:**
- ✅ Fast prototyping
- ✅ Consistent design

**Cons:**
- ❌ HTML gets messy with 10+ classes
- ❌ Learning curve (memorize class names)
- ❌ Extra build step needed

**My choice for Sonorum:** **Plain CSS**

Why? You want precise control for music notation. Frameworks are for generic 
websites.

---

## **Part 10: Testing**

### What is Testing?

Writing code to **check if your code works**.

**Example:**
```javascript
// Code to test
function add(a, b) {
  return a + b;
}

// Test
console.assert(add(2, 3) === 5, 'add(2,3) should equal 5');
```

If test fails, you get an error. If it passes, silence.

---

### Testing Frameworks (Vitest, Jest)

**What are they?**
Tools that make testing easier.

**Without framework:**
```javascript
console.assert(add(2, 3) === 5);
console.assert(add(0, 0) === 0);
console.assert(add(-1, 1) === 0);
```

**With Vitest:**
```javascript
import { test, expect } from 'vitest';

test('add function', () => {
  expect(add(2, 3)).toBe(5);
  expect(add(0, 0)).toBe(0);
  expect(add(-1, 1)).toBe(0);
});
```

Nicer output, better organization.

---

### **My Choice: Manual Testing First**

**Now:**
```javascript
// In browser console
import { getScale } from './core/theory.js';
console.log(getScale('C', 'Major'));
// ['C', 'D', 'E', 'F', 'G', 'A', 'B']
```

**Later (when you have 20+ functions):**
```bash
npm install -D vitest
npm run test
```

Testing is important, but manual testing works fine initially.

## **Python TUI Version - What It Would Look Like**

### Using Textual (Modern Python TUI Framework)

**Installation:**
```bash
pip install textual
```

**Simple example:**
```python
from textual.app import App, ComposeResult
from textual.widgets import Header, Footer, Input, Button
from textual.containers import Container

class SonorumApp(App):
    CSS = """
    Container {
        layout: vertical;
        padding: 1;
    }
    """
    
    def compose(self) -> ComposeResult:
        yield Header()
        yield Container(
            Input(placeholder="Song Title"),
            Input(placeholder="Key"),
            Input(placeholder="Tempo"),
            Button("Add Instrument", id="add_inst")
        )
        yield Footer()
    
    def on_button_pressed(self, event):
        if event.button.id == "add_inst":
            self.notify("Adding instrument...")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    app = SonorumApp()
    app.run()
```

**Run:**
```bash
python sonorum.py
```

Boom. Runs in terminal. No browser.

---

### 32-Column Tab in Python TUI:

```python
def render_tab(notes, tuning=['E','B','G','D','A','E']):
    """Render 32-column tab in terminal"""
    
    # Build grid
    grid = {string: ['-'] * 32 for string in tuning}
    
    # Place notes
    for note in notes:
        col = (note['beat'] * 8) + note['subdivision']
        grid[tuning[note['string']]][col] = str(note['fret'])
    
    # Render
    output = []
    for string in tuning:
        line = f"{string} │ " + ''.join(grid[string]) + " │"
        output.append(line)
    
    # Add rhythm notation
    rhythm = '  │ ' + (' ' * 32) + ' │'  # Simplified
    output.append(rhythm)
    
    return '\n'.join(output)
```

**Output:**
```
E │ -------------------------------- │
B │ -------------------------------- │
G │ -------------------------------- │
D │ --------0-------0--------------- │
A │ --------2-------2--------------- │
E │ 2------------------------------- │
  │                                  │
```

**It works!** Not as pretty as Unicode musical symbols, but functional.

### **Python TUI:**
**Best for:**
- Terminal lovers (you work in terminal anyway)
- Quick composition workflow
- Single-purpose tool (just composition, no bells/whistles)
- Python ecosystem comfort

**Example:** Vim (terminal-based, incredibly powerful)

**Demo:**
```python
from textual.app import App
from textual.widgets import DataTable

class TabEditor(App):
    def compose(self):
        yield DataTable()
    
    def on_mount(self):
        table = self.query_one(DataTable)
        table.add_columns(*"EBGDAE")
        table.add_rows([
            ["-", "-", "-", "-", "-", "2"],
            ["-", "-", "-", "0", "2", "-"],
            # ... 32 columns total
        ])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    TabEditor().run()
```

Actually pretty powerful!

## **Code Sharing Between TUI and Web**

**Python backend (shared):**
```python
# core/project.py
class Project:
    def __init__(self):
        self.metadata = {}
        self.instruments = []
    
    def to_json(self):
        return json.dumps(self.__dict__)
    
    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, data):
        return cls(**json.loads(data))
```

**TUI uses it directly:**
```python
from core.project import Project
project = Project()
```

**Web uses it via API:**
```python
# api.py
from flask import Flask, jsonify
from core.project import Project

app = Flask(__name__)

@app.route('/api/project')
def get_project():
    project = Project.load('current.son')
    return jsonify(project.to_json())
```

**Same logic, multiple frontends!**

LordFingers: Ok yeah. I can see this being better in a more graphically beautiful state. Is 
there an equivalent that is desktop based as opposed to html?

Claude:  ## Desktop App Options - Native vs Web-Based

Excellent question. Let's explore **true desktop applications** vs. 
web-wrapped-as-desktop.

---

## **Two Philosophies**

### **1. Native Desktop** (Platform-specific)
Built with platform-specific tools. Looks and feels like a "real" desktop app.

- **Windows**: C#/.NET (WPF, WinForms)
- **macOS**: Swift (AppKit, SwiftUI)
- **Linux**: C++ (Qt, GTK)

**Pros:**
- ✅ Native look/feel (matches OS perfectly)
- ✅ Best performance
- ✅ Deep OS integration (file system, notifications)
- ✅ No browser needed

**Cons:**
- ❌ **Must build 3 separate apps** (Windows, Mac, Linux)
- ❌ **Different languages** for each platform
- ❌ **3x the work** to maintain
- ❌ Learning curve for each platform

---

### **2. Web Technology as Desktop** (Cross-platform)
Write once in HTML/CSS/JS, runs as desktop app.

**Main Options:**
- **Electron** (most popular)
- **Tauri** (modern, lightweight)
- **NW.js** (older Electron alternative)

**Pros:**
- ✅ Write once, runs everywhere
- ✅ One codebase for Mac/Win/Linux
- ✅ Use web skills (HTML/CSS/JS)
- ✅ Rich UI capabilities

**Cons:**
- ❌ Larger file size (bundles Chromium browser)
- ❌ More RAM usage
- ❌ "Not quite native" feel

---

## **The Best Desktop Options for Sonorum**

### **Option 1: Electron** (Most Popular)

**What is Electron?**
Wraps your web app in a desktop window. Uses Chromium (Chrome's engine) + 
Node.js.

**Famous apps built with Electron:**
- VS Code (Microsoft)
- Discord
- Slack
- Spotify desktop
- GitHub Desktop
- Obsidian
- Figma

Basically **the industry standard** for desktop apps using web tech.

---

**How it works:**
```javascript
// main.js (Electron entry point)
const { app, BrowserWindow } = require('electron');

function createWindow() {
  const win = new BrowserWindow({
    width: 1200,
    height: 800,
    webPreferences: {
      nodeIntegration: true
    }
  });
  
  win.loadFile('index.html'); // Your web app
}

app.whenReady().then(createWindow);
```

That's it. Now your web app is a desktop app.

**File size:** ~150-200 MB (includes entire Chrome browser)
**RAM usage:** ~100-200 MB
**Startup:** ~1-2 seconds

---

**Pros:**
✅ **Huge ecosystem** - Tons of tutorials, libraries
✅ **Same code as web version** - Build web app, wrap it in Electron
✅ **Native menus** - File menu, keyboard shortcuts, dock integration
✅ **File system access** - Save files anywhere
✅ **Auto-updates** - Built-in updater
✅ **Code signing** - Can notarize for Mac, sign for Windows
✅ **Offline-first** - No internet needed

**Cons:**
❌ **Large downloads** - Each app is ~150 MB
❌ **RAM hungry** - Runs a full browser
❌ **"Not native"** - Users can tell it's a web app
❌ **Security concerns** - Node.js in renderer is risky (fixable)

---

### **Option 2: Tauri** (Modern Alternative)

**What is Tauri?**
Like Electron, but uses the **OS's built-in browser** instead of bundling 
Chromium.

**How it's different:**
- **Electron**: Bundles Chrome → 150 MB app
- **Tauri**: Uses Safari (Mac), Edge (Windows), WebKit (Linux) → **3-5 MB 
app**

**Backend in Rust** (super fast, secure)
**Frontend in web tech** (HTML/CSS/JS)

---

**Comparison:**

| Feature | Electron | Tauri |
|---------|----------|-------|
| App size | 150 MB | 3-5 MB |
| RAM usage | 100-200 MB | 50-80 MB |
| Performance | Good | Excellent |
| Maturity | Proven (2013) | Newer (2020) |
| Ecosystem | Huge | Growing |
| Learning curve | Easy | Medium (Rust) |

---

**Tauri example:**
```rust
// src-tauri/main.rs
#[tauri::command]
fn save_project(data: String) -> Result<String, String> {
    std::fs::write("project.son", data)
        .map(|_| "Saved".to_string())
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())
}

fn main() {
    tauri::Builder::default()
        .invoke_handler(tauri::generate_handler![save_project])
        .run(tauri::generate_context!())
        .expect("error while running tauri");
}
```

```javascript
// Frontend (your web app)
import { invoke } from '@tauri-apps/api';

async function saveProject(data) {
  await invoke('save_project', { data });
}
```

**Pros:**
✅ **Tiny apps** - 3-5 MB vs. 150 MB
✅ **Fast** - Native performance
✅ **Secure** - Rust backend is memory-safe
✅ **Modern** - Active development
✅ **Less RAM** - Uses OS browser

**Cons:**
❌ **Newer** - Less mature than Electron
❌ **Rust** - Need to learn Rust for backend
❌ **Smaller ecosystem** - Fewer plugins
❌ **Browser differences** - Safari vs. Edge might render slightly different

---

### **Option 3: Qt + Python (PyQt/PySide)**

**What is Qt?**
A **truly native** cross-platform framework. Powers apps like:
- Autodesk Maya
- VirtualBox
- Telegram Desktop
- OBS Studio

**With Python bindings (PyQt6 or PySide6):**

```python
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QApplication, QMainWindow, QPushButton

class Sonorum(QMainWindow):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.setWindowTitle("Sonorum")
        
        button = QPushButton("Add Instrument")
        button.clicked.connect(self.add_instrument)
        self.setCentralWidget(button)
    
    def add_instrument(self):
        print("Adding instrument...")

app = QApplication([])
window = Sonorum()
window.show()
app.exec()
```

**Pros:**
✅ **True native** - Looks like a real desktop app
✅ **Python** - You already know Python
✅ **Cross-platform** - Write once, native everywhere
✅ **Small binaries** - 20-40 MB
✅ **Fast** - C++ under the hood
✅ **Mature** - Been around since 1995

**Cons:**
❌ **Learning curve** - Qt is complex
❌ **Not web tech** - Can't reuse HTML/CSS skills
❌ **Less flexible UI** - Harder than CSS for custom designs
❌ **Licensing** - LGPL (or commercial license)

---

### **Option 4: Flutter Desktop**

**What is Flutter?**
Google's UI framework. Originally for mobile, now does desktop.

**Language:** Dart (similar to JavaScript/Java)

```dart
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';

void main() {
  runApp(SonorumApp());
}

class SonorumApp extends StatelessWidget {
  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return MaterialApp(
      home: Scaffold(
        appBar: AppBar(title: Text('Sonorum')),
        body: Center(
          child: ElevatedButton(
            onPressed: () => print('Add Instrument'),
            child: Text('Add Instrument'),
          ),
        ),
      ),
    );
  }
}
```

**Pros:**
✅ **Beautiful UI** - Animated, smooth
✅ **Cross-platform** - Mobile + Desktop from one code
✅ **Fast** - Compiles to native
✅ **Hot reload** - See changes instantly

**Cons:**
❌ **New language** - Must learn Dart
❌ **Desktop is beta** - Still maturing
❌ **Large apps** - 30-50 MB
❌ **Not web-based** - Different paradigm

---

## **Head-to-Head Comparison**

### For Sonorum Specifically:

| Criteria | Electron | Tauri | Qt/Python | Flutter |
|----------|----------|-------|-----------|---------|
| **Use web skills** | ✅ Yes | ✅ Yes | ❌ No | ❌ No |
| **App size** | ❌ 150 MB | ✅ 5 MB | ✅ 40 MB | ⚠️ 50 MB |
| **RAM usage** | ❌ High | ✅ Low | ✅ Low | ✅ Low |
| **Learning curve** | ✅ Easy | ⚠️ Medium | ❌ Hard | ❌ Hard |
| **Music notation** | ✅ Perfect | ✅ Perfect | ⚠️ Doable | ⚠️ 
Doable |
| **PDF export** | ✅ Easy | ✅ Easy | ⚠️ Medium | ⚠️ Medium |
| **Native feel** | ❌ Meh | ⚠️ Better | ✅ Perfect | ✅ Perfect |
| **Cross-platform** | ✅ Easy | ✅ Easy | ✅ Easy | ✅ Easy |
| **Maturity** | ✅ Proven | ⚠️ New | ✅ Proven | ⚠️ Growing |

---

## **My Recommendation for Sonorum**

### **Go with Electron (or Tauri if you want modern)**

**Why Electron:**

1. **Reuse your web code** - Everything we planned (HTML/CSS/JS) still works
2. **Rich graphics** - Perfect for musical notation, fretboards
3. **PDF export** - Browser print works perfectly
4. **You're learning anyway** - Might as well learn the industry standard
5. **Massive ecosystem** - Every problem is already solved

**Why NOT Qt/Flutter:**
- You'd have to rebuild the UI from scratch (no HTML/CSS)
- Musical notation would be harder (no Unicode support in standard widgets)
- PDF export more complex
- Steeper learning curve

---

## **The Electron Path (Recommended)**

### Structure:
```
sonorum/
├── package.json           # Dependencies
├── main.js               # Electron entry (creates window)
├── preload.js            # Security bridge
├── index.html            # Your app (same as web version)
├── src/                  # Your modules (same as web version)
│   ├── state/
│   ├── core/
│   ├── pages/
│   └── ...
└── styles/               # CSS (same as web version)
```

**The magic:** Your web app code **doesn't change**. You just wrap it.

---

### Quick Start:

```bash
npm init -y
npm install electron --save-dev
```

**main.js:**
```javascript
const { app, BrowserWindow, Menu } = require('electron');
const path = require('path');

function createWindow() {
  const win = new BrowserWindow({
    width: 1400,
    height: 900,
    webPreferences: {
      preload: path.join(__dirname, 'preload.js'),
      contextIsolation: true
    }
  });
  
  // Load your web app
  win.loadFile('index.html');
  
  // Open DevTools in development
  if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development') {
    win.webContents.openDevTools();
  }
  
  // Create custom menu
  const menu = Menu.buildFromTemplate([
    {
      label: 'File',
      submenu: [
        {
          label: 'New Project',
          accelerator: 'CmdOrCtrl+N',
          click: () => win.webContents.send('new-project')
        },
        {
          label: 'Save',
          accelerator: 'CmdOrCtrl+S',
          click: () => win.webContents.send('save-project')
        },
        { type: 'separator' },
        { role: 'quit' }
      ]
    },
    {
      label: 'Edit',
      submenu: [
        { role: 'undo' },
        { role: 'redo' },
        { type: 'separator' },
        { role: 'cut' },
        { role: 'copy' },
        { role: 'paste' }
      ]
    }
  ]);
  Menu.setApplicationMenu(menu);
}

app.whenReady().then(createWindow);

app.on('window-all-closed', () => {
  if (process.platform !== 'darwin') {
    app.quit();
  }
});
```

**package.json:**
```json
{
  "name": "sonorum",
  "version": "2.0.0",
  "main": "main.js",
  "scripts": {
    "start": "electron .",
    "dev": "NODE_ENV=development electron .",
    "build": "electron-builder"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "electron": "^28.0.0",
    "electron-builder": "^24.0.0"
  }
}
```

**Run:**
```bash
npm start
```

Boom. Desktop app.

---

### Building for Distribution:

```bash
npm install --save-dev electron-builder
npm run build
```

Creates:
- **Mac**: `Sonorum.app` (or `.dmg`)
- **Windows**: `Sonorum.exe` (or installer)
- **Linux**: `.AppImage` or `.deb`

One command, all platforms.

**Nearly identical to Electron** from your perspective, just:

## **File System Access (Big Advantage)**

### Web browser:
```javascript
// User must click "Save" dialog
const blob = new Blob([data], { type: 'application/json' });
const url = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
const a = document.createElement('a');
a.href = url;
a.download = 'project.son';
a.click(); // ugh
```

### Electron:
```javascript
// preload.js exposes file API
const { ipcRenderer } = require('electron');

window.electronAPI = {
  saveFile: (path, data) => ipcRenderer.invoke('save-file', path, data),
  openFile: (path) => ipcRenderer.invoke('open-file', path)
};

// In your app
await window.electronAPI.saveFile('/Users/you/song.son', projectData);
```

**Direct file system access!** No browser security restrictions.

- Create `main.js`

- ✅ Native feel (menus, file system, etc.)

**Downsides:**

But for Sonorum? **Totally worth it.**

## **New Recommendation: Python + Qt (PyQt6 or PySide6)**

### Why This is Perfect for You:

✅ **Native Linux app** - Looks and feels like a proper desktop application

## **What Qt Gives You**

Qt (pronounced "cute") is a **mature, powerful GUI framework**.

### You can build:
- Complex layouts (grids, splitters, tabs)
- Custom widgets (32-column tab grid, fretboard)
- Rich text rendering (musical notation with fonts)
- Graphics scenes (interactive fretboard, chord diagrams)
- Native file dialogs
- Keyboard shortcuts
- Menus and toolbars
- Print/PDF export

**Basically everything Sonorum needs.**

## **Python + Qt: What It Looks Like**

### Installation:
```bash
pip install PySide6
# or
pip install PyQt6
```

(PySide6 is officially maintained by Qt, PyQt6 is third-party but older/more 
docs)

---

### Minimal Example:

```python
from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QApplication, QMainWindow, QWidget, QVBoxLayout,
    QLabel, QLineEdit, QPushButton, QTabWidget
)
from PySide6.QtCore import Qt

class SonorumWindow(QMainWindow):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.setWindowTitle("Sonorum v2")
        self.setGeometry(100, 100, 1200, 800)
        
        # Central widget
        central = QWidget()
        self.setCentralWidget(central)
        
        # Layout
        layout = QVBoxLayout()
        central.setLayout(layout)
        
        # Title input
        title_input = QLineEdit()
        title_input.setPlaceholderText("Song Title")
        layout.addWidget(QLabel("Song Title:"))
        layout.addWidget(title_input)
        
        # Tempo input
        tempo_input = QLineEdit()
        tempo_input.setPlaceholderText("120")
        layout.addWidget(QLabel("Tempo (BPM):"))
        layout.addWidget(tempo_input)
        
        # Button
        btn = QPushButton("Add Instrument")
        btn.clicked.connect(self.add_instrument)
        layout.addWidget(btn)
    
    def add_instrument(self):
        print("Adding instrument...")

if __name__ == '__main__':
    app = QApplication([])
    window = SonorumWindow()
    window.show()
    app.exec()
```

**Run:**
```bash
python sonorum.py
```

Opens a proper window with native Linux styling.

---

## **Tab Widget Example (Your 32-Column Grid)**

Qt has `QTableWidget` perfect for this:

```python
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QTableWidget, QTableWidgetItem

class TabEditor(QWidget):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        
        layout = QVBoxLayout()
        self.setLayout(layout)
        
        # Create 32-column table
        self.table = QTableWidget(6, 32)  # 6 strings, 32 columns
        
        # Set row labels (string names)
        self.table.setVerticalHeaderLabels(['E', 'B', 'G', 'D', 'A', 'E'])
        
        # Set column labels (bar positions)
        self.table.setHorizontalHeaderLabels([str(i) for i in range(32)])
        
        # Fill with dashes
        for row in range(6):
            for col in range(32):
                self.table.setItem(row, col, QTableWidgetItem('-'))
        
        # Add note
        self.table.item(5, 0).setText('2')  # Low E, position 0, fret 2
        
        layout.addWidget(self.table)

```

**Result:** Proper grid with cells you can click, edit, style.

---

## **Custom Graphics (Fretboard, Diagrams)**

Qt has `QGraphicsScene` for custom 2D graphics:

```python
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QGraphicsView, QGraphicsScene
from PySide6.QtCore import QRectF
from PySide6.QtGui import QPen, QBrush, QColor

class Fretboard(QGraphicsView):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        
        self.scene = QGraphicsScene()
        self.setScene(self.scene)
        
        # Draw frets
        for fret in range(23):
            x = fret * 60
            self.scene.addLine(x, 0, x, 300, QPen(QColor('gray')))
        
        # Draw strings
        for string in range(6):
            y = string * 50
            self.scene.addLine(0, y, 1320, y, QPen(QColor('black')))
        
        # Draw note (circle at fret 5, string 0)
        self.scene.addEllipse(
            QRectF(5 * 60 - 10, 0 * 50 - 10, 20, 20),
            QPen(QColor('blue')),
            QBrush(QColor('lightblue'))
        )
```

**Full control** over rendering. Can make it look exactly how you want.

---

## **Musical Notation (Unicode Symbols)**

Qt supports Unicode perfectly:

```python
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QLabel
from PySide6.QtGui import QFont

# Rhythm notation label
rhythm_label = QLabel("𝅘𝅥 𝅘𝅥𝅮 𝅘𝅥𝅯")  # Quarter, eighth, 
sixteenth
rhythm_label.setFont(QFont("Arial", 24))  # Or use a music font

# Bar lines
bar_label = QLabel("𝄕 𝄃 -------------------------------- 𝄂")
```

Works natively. No browser rendering issues.

---

## **File Menu (Native)**

```python
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QMenuBar, QFileDialog
from PySide6.QtGui import QAction

class SonorumWindow(QMainWindow):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        
        # Create menu bar
        menubar = self.menuBar()
        
        # File menu
        file_menu = menubar.addMenu('File')
        
        # New action
        new_action = QAction('New Project', self)
        new_action.setShortcut('Ctrl+N')
        new_action.triggered.connect(self.new_project)
        file_menu.addAction(new_action)
        
        # Save action
        save_action = QAction('Save', self)
        save_action.setShortcut('Ctrl+S')
        save_action.triggered.connect(self.save_project)
        file_menu.addAction(save_action)
        
        # Open action
        open_action = QAction('Open...', self)
        open_action.setShortcut('Ctrl+O')
        open_action.triggered.connect(self.open_project)
        file_menu.addAction(open_action)
    
    def save_project(self):
        filename, _ = QFileDialog.getSaveFileName(
            self,
            "Save Project",
            "",
            "Sonorum Files (*.son);;All Files (*)"
        )
        if filename:
            # Save your project data
            with open(filename, 'w') as f:
                f.write(json.dumps(self.project_data))
    
    def open_project(self):
        filename, _ = QFileDialog.getOpenFileName(
            self,
            "Open Project",
            "",
            "Sonorum Files (*.son);;All Files (*)"
        )
        if filename:
            with open(filename, 'r') as f:
                self.project_data = json.load(f)
```

**Native file dialogs!** Looks like every other Linux app.

---

## **PDF Export**

Qt has built-in PDF generation:

```python
from PySide6.QtGui import QPdfWriter, QPainter
from PySide6.QtCore import QRectF

def export_pdf(filename):
    writer = QPdfWriter(filename)
    writer.setPageSize(QPageSize.PageSizeId.A4)
    
    painter = QPainter(writer)
    
    # Draw your tab notation
    painter.drawText(100, 100, "Sonorum - Guitar Tab")
    painter.drawText(100, 200, "E |--------------------------------|")
    painter.drawText(100, 250, "B |--------------------------------|")
    # ... etc
    
    painter.end()
```

Or use ReportLab for more complex layouts:

```python
from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter
from reportlab.pdfgen import canvas

def export_pdf(filename, project_data):
    c = canvas.Canvas(filename, pagesize=letter)
    c.drawString(100, 750, f"Song: {project_data['title']}")
    c.drawString(100, 730, f"Tempo: {project_data['tempo']} BPM")
    # Render tab...
    c.save()
```

```python
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QTabWidget

class SonorumWindow(QMainWindow):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        
        # Tab widget
        tabs = QTabWidget()
        self.setCentralWidget(tabs)
        
        # Add pages
        tabs.addTab(SongCorePage(), "Song Core")
        tabs.addTab(MasterCompositionPage(), "Master Composition")
        tabs.addTab(TabEditorPage(), "Lead Guitar")
        tabs.addTab(TabEditorPage(), "Bass")
        tabs.addTab(DrumsPage(), "Drums")
        tabs.addTab(LyricsPage(), "Lyrics")
```

Each tab is a separate `QWidget` - perfect for your modular page system.

```
    ├── icons/              # UI icons
```

**Same modular structure**, just Python instead of JavaScript.

```python
import sys
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QApplication, QMainWindow, QLabel

class Sonorum(QMainWindow):
```

## **Two Input Modes**

### Mode 1: **Simple Text Entry**
Just type lyrics, system places chords automatically based on Master 
Composition.

```
Type your lyrics:
_____________________________________________
Midnight rambler, walking down the street
Looking for a rhythm, feeling the beat
Guitar in hand, ready to play
Making music all night and day
_____________________________________________

[Auto-place chords from Master Composition]
```

System reads chord progression from Master Composition and places them above 
the text at bar boundaries.

---

### Mode 2: **Manual Chord Placement**
Click where you want chord names to appear:

```
        |Dm                   |Am
Midnight rambler, walking down the street
        |Bb                   |C
Looking for a rhythm, feeling the beat
```

Click between words → Dropdown appears → Choose chord → Places it inline.

---

## **Features**

### ✅ **Inherits from Master Composition**
- Chord progression comes from Master Composition
- But you can override/customize per section
- Example: Master says "Dm - Am - Bb - C", but verse 2 might be "Dm - Am - F - 
C"

### ✅ **Automatic Syllable Alignment** (Future)
Show rhythm subdivision under each syllable:
```
Mid-night ram-bler, walk-ing down the street
♩   ♪   ♪  ♪      ♪   ♪   ♪    ♪  ♪
```

### ✅ **Multiple Vocal Parts** (Future)
- Lead vocals
- Backing vocals
- Harmonies (shown as additional lines)

### ✅ **Section Repeats**
```
[Verse 1]
Midnight rambler...

[Verse 2 - same chords, different lyrics]
City lights are...

[Chorus]
We're gonna rock all night...
```

### ✅ **Export Options**
- **Print with chords** - Singer sees chord names
- **Print lyrics only** - Clean lyrics sheet
- **Export to text file** - .txt with chords inline

---

## **Data Structure**

```python
@dataclass
class LyricLine:
    """Single line of lyrics with chord placements"""
    text: str
    chord_placements: List[ChordPlacement] = field(default_factory=list)

@dataclass
class ChordPlacement:
    """Chord placed at specific position in lyrics"""
    position: int  # Character index in text
    chord: str     # Chord name (e.g., "Dm")

@dataclass
class VocalSection:
    """Vocals for a section"""
    section_id: str
    lines: List[LyricLine]
    use_master_chords: bool = True  # Inherit from Master Composition?
    custom_chords: List[ChordChange] = field(default_factory=list)
```

---

## **UI Implementation (Qt)**

### Widget: `LyricsEditor` (QTextEdit with custom behavior)

```python
class LyricsEditor(QTextEdit):
    """Rich text editor for lyrics with chord placement"""
    
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.chord_positions = []  # List of (position, chord)
    
    def insert_chord(self, chord_name):
        """Insert chord at cursor position"""
        cursor = self.textCursor()
        position = cursor.position()
        
        # Insert chord in smaller font above text
        cursor.insertHtml(f'<span style="color: #3b82f6; font-size: 
10px;">{chord_name}</span><br>')
        
        self.chord_positions.append((position, chord_name))
    
    def auto_place_chords(self, chord_progression, bars_per_line):
        """Automatically place chords based on Master Composition"""
        text = self.toPlainText()
        lines = text.split('\n')
        
        # Place chords at bar boundaries
        # (simplified logic)
        for i, line in enumerate(lines):
            bar_in_section = i // bars_per_line
            if bar_in_section < len(chord_progression):
                chord = chord_progression[bar_in_section].chord
                # Insert chord at line start
```

### Visual Display:

Uses **HTML formatting** in QTextEdit:
```html
<p>
  <span style="color: #3b82f6; font-weight: bold;">Dm</span><br>
  Midnight rambler, walking down the street
</p>
```

Or uses **custom painting** in QGraphicsScene for precise positioning.

---

## **Integration with Master Composition**

### Scenario 1: **Master Composition Defines Chords**

Master Composition says:
- Verse 1: Dm → Am → Bb → C (8 bars, 2 bars per chord)

Lyrics page:
1. Opens Verse 1
2. Sees 4 chord markers (Dm, Am, Bb, C)
3. Places them automatically at appropriate positions
4. You just type lyrics between markers

### Scenario 2: **Custom Chord Placement**

You toggle "Custom chords for this section":
- Master chords are dimmed (suggestions)
- You manually place chords wherever you want
- Useful for verses with different chord rhythms

---

## **Comparison to Guitar Pro / Other Tools**

### **Guitar Pro Lyrics:**
```
Shows chords above staff
Lyrics below staff
Synced to tab rhythm
```

### **Sonorum Lyrics:**
```
Shows chords inline with text (more readable for singers)
Inherits from Master Composition (consistency)
Optional rhythm notation under syllables
Can be independent or synced to instruments
```

---

Show pitch contour above lyrics:

```
     ●―●―●

  ●
Mid-night ram-bler
```

For complex words:
```
Supercalifragilisticexpialidocious
(soo-per-cal-ih-fraj-ih-lis-tik-ex-pee-al-ih-doh-shus)
```

### **Multi-Language Support**
Toggle romanization for non-Latin scripts:
```
こんにちは
(Konnichiwa)
```

### **Rhyme Highlighting**
Highlight rhyme scheme:
```
Walking down the street  (A)
Feeling the beat        (A)
Ready to play          (B)
All night and day      (B)
```

## **File to Create**

### `ui/pages/lyrics_editor.py`

```python
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QWidget, QVBoxLayout, QTextEdit, QPushButton
from PySide6.QtCore import Signal

class LyricsEditorPage(QWidget):
    """Lyrics editor with chord placement"""
    
    lyrics_changed = Signal()
    
    def __init__(self, main_window):
        super().__init__()
        self.main_window = main_window
        self.current_section = None
        
        self.setup_ui()
    
    def setup_ui(self):
        layout = QVBoxLayout()
        
        # Chord display (from Master Composition)
        self.chord_bar = QLabel()
        layout.addWidget(self.chord_bar)
        
        # Lyrics text editor
        self.lyrics_editor = QTextEdit()
        self.lyrics_editor.setPlaceholderText("Enter lyrics here...")
        layout.addWidget(self.lyrics_editor)
        
        # Buttons
        btn_layout = QHBoxLayout()
        
        auto_chord_btn = QPushButton("Auto-place Chords")
        auto_chord_btn.clicked.connect(self.auto_place_chords)
        btn_layout.addWidget(auto_chord_btn)
        
        insert_chord_btn = QPushButton("Insert Chord")
        insert_chord_btn.clicked.connect(self.insert_chord_at_cursor)
        btn_layout.addWidget(insert_chord_btn)
        
        layout.addLayout(btn_layout)
        self.setLayout(layout)
```

## Here's the structure:

### `master_composition.py` (the page)
The full page you see when you click the "Master Composition" tab. It has:
- Left panel: Section list
- Right panel: Chord progression editor ← **This needed a widget**

### `chord_progression_editor.py` (the widget)
A **reusable widget** that shows the chord grid. It's embedded inside the 
Master Composition page's right panel.

```
┌─ Master Composition Page 
─────────────────────────┐
│                                                    │
│  ┌─ Section List ──┐  ┌─ Chord Progression ────┐ │
│  │ Verse 1         │  │ ← This is the widget   │ │
│  │ Chorus          │  │                         │ │
│  │ Bridge          │  │ Bar 1: [Dm ▼]           │ │
│  │                 │  │ Bar 2: [Am ▼]           │ │
│  │ [Add Section]   │  │ Bar 3: [Bb ▼]           │ │
│  └─────────────────┘  │ Bar 4: [C  
▼]           │ │
│                       │                         │ │
│                       │ [Auto-fill] [Clear]     │ │
│                       
└─────────────────────────�
� │
└─────────────────────────�
�──────────────────────────�
��
```

## Why separate files?

**Good design pattern:**
- `master_composition.py` = Controller (page logic)
- `chord_progression_editor.py` = View (reusable UI widget)

This widget could be reused elsewhere (like in a popup chord editor, or in 
instrument pages to show current chords).

## Files you have now:

```
ui/pages/
├── song_core.py              ← Full page
├── master_composition.py     ← Full page (uses chord widget)
└── lyrics_editor.py          ← (placeholder, to build later)

ui/widgets/
└── chord_progression_editor.py  ← Reusable widget

ui/dialogs/
├── instrument_dialog.py      ← Popup dialog
└── section_dialog.py         ← Popup dialog
```

**Nothing renamed** - just created the chord editor as a separate widget for 
cleaner code!

Does that make sense? It's all working together in the Master Composition page.

```python
self.master_comp_page = MasterCompositionPage(self)

self.tabs.addTab(self.master_comp_page, "Master Composition")
```

**Line 91 - Load project into it:**
```python
self.song_core_page.load_project(self.current_project)
self.master_comp_page.load_project(self.current_project)
```

**Line 111 - Same for open_project:**
```python
self.song_core_page.load_project(self.current_project)
self.master_comp_page.load_project(self.current_project)
```

### File 1: `ui/pages/master_composition.py`

Copy this entire file:

```python
"""
Sonorum - Master Composition Page
Define song structure, sections, and chord progressions
"""

from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QWidget, QVBoxLayout, QHBoxLayout, QGroupBox,
    QPushButton, QListWidget, QListWidgetItem, QLabel,
    QMessageBox, QSplitter
)
from PySide6.QtCore import Qt, Signal
import uuid

from core.project import Project, Section, ChordChange
from ui.dialogs.section_dialog import SectionDialog
from ui.widgets.chord_progression_editor import ChordProgressionEditor


class MasterCompositionPage(QWidget):
    """Master Composition page - song structure and chord progressions"""
    
    # Signals
    section_added = Signal(Section)
    section_removed = Signal(str)  # section_id
    section_modified = Signal(Section)
    
    def __init__(self, main_window):
        super().__init__()
        self.main_window = main_window
        self.current_project = None
        self.selected_section = None
        
        self.setup_ui()
    
    def setup_ui(self):
        """Setup user interface"""
        # Main horizontal splitter
        splitter = QSplitter(Qt.Horizontal)
        
        # Left panel: Section list
        left_panel = self.create_section_list_panel()
        splitter.addWidget(left_panel)
        
        # Right panel: Chord progression editor
        right_panel = self.create_chord_editor_panel()
        splitter.addWidget(right_panel)
        
        # Set splitter proportions (40% left, 60% right)
        splitter.setStretchFactor(0, 2)
        splitter.setStretchFactor(1, 3)
        
        # Main layout
        layout = QVBoxLayout()
        layout.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        
        # Title bar
        title_bar = QHBoxLayout()
        title = QLabel("🎼 MASTER COMPOSITION")
        title.setStyleSheet("font-size: 20px; font-weight: bold; color: 
#3b82f6; padding: 15px;")
        title_bar.addWidget(title)
        title_bar.addStretch()
        
        layout.addLayout(title_bar)
        layout.addWidget(splitter)
        
        self.setLayout(layout)
    
    def create_section_list_panel(self):
        """Create left panel with section list"""
        panel = QWidget()
        layout = QVBoxLayout()
        panel.setLayout(layout)
        
        # Section list group
        group = QGroupBox("Song Structure")
        group_layout = QVBoxLayout()
        
        # Section list
        self.section_list = QListWidget()
        self.section_list.setSelectionMode(QListWidget.SingleSelection)
        
self.section_list.itemSelectionChanged.connect(self.on_section_selection_changed
)
        group_layout.addWidget(self.section_list)
        
        # Timeline visualization (simplified)
        self.timeline_label = QLabel()
        self.timeline_label.setWordWrap(True)
        self.timeline_label.setStyleSheet("""
            background-color: #2a2a2a;
            border: 1px solid #444;
            padding: 8px;
            font-family: monospace;
            font-size: 11px;
        """)
        group_layout.addWidget(self.timeline_label)
        
        # Buttons
        button_layout = QHBoxLayout()
        
        add_btn = QPushButton("+ Add Section")
        add_btn.clicked.connect(self.add_section)
        button_layout.addWidget(add_btn)
        
        self.edit_btn = QPushButton("Edit")
        self.edit_btn.clicked.connect(self.edit_section)
        self.edit_btn.setEnabled(False)
        button_layout.addWidget(self.edit_btn)
        
        self.duplicate_btn = QPushButton("Duplicate")
        self.duplicate_btn.clicked.connect(self.duplicate_section)
        self.duplicate_btn.setEnabled(False)
        button_layout.addWidget(self.duplicate_btn)
        
        self.remove_btn = QPushButton("Remove")
        self.remove_btn.clicked.connect(self.remove_section)
        self.remove_btn.setEnabled(False)
        button_layout.addWidget(self.remove_btn)
        
        group_layout.addLayout(button_layout)
        
        # Move up/down buttons
        move_layout = QHBoxLayout()
        
        self.move_up_btn = QPushButton("↑ Move Up")
        self.move_up_btn.clicked.connect(self.move_section_up)
        self.move_up_btn.setEnabled(False)
        move_layout.addWidget(self.move_up_btn)
        
        self.move_down_btn = QPushButton("↓ Move Down")
        self.move_down_btn.clicked.connect(self.move_section_down)
        self.move_down_btn.setEnabled(False)
        move_layout.addWidget(self.move_down_btn)
        
        group_layout.addLayout(move_layout)
        
        group.setLayout(group_layout)
        layout.addWidget(group)
        
        return panel
    
    def create_chord_editor_panel(self):
        """Create right panel with chord progression editor"""
        panel = QWidget()
        layout = QVBoxLayout()
        panel.setLayout(layout)
        
        # Info group
        self.info_group = QGroupBox("Selected Section")
        info_layout = QVBoxLayout()
        
        self.section_info_label = QLabel("No section selected")
        self.section_info_label.setStyleSheet("font-size: 14px; padding: 10px;")
        info_layout.addWidget(self.section_info_label)
        
        self.info_group.setLayout(info_layout)
        layout.addWidget(self.info_group)
        
        # Chord progression editor
        self.chord_editor = ChordProgressionEditor(self)
        
self.chord_editor.chord_progression_changed.connect(self.on_chord_progression_ch
anged)
        layout.addWidget(self.chord_editor)
        
        return panel
    
    def load_project(self, project: Project):
        """Load project data"""
        self.current_project = project
        self.refresh_section_list()
        self.update_timeline()
    
    def refresh_section_list(self):
        """Refresh section list display"""
        self.section_list.clear()
        
        if not self.current_project:
            return
        
        for section in self.current_project.master_composition.sections:
            # Create display text
            text = self.format_section_display(section)
            
            item = QListWidgetItem(text)
            item.setData(Qt.UserRole, section.id)
            self.section_list.addItem(item)
    
    def format_section_display(self, section):
        """Format section for list display"""
        # Icon based on type
        icons = {
            'Intro': '▶',
            'Verse': '📝',
            'Chorus': '🎵',
            'Bridge': '🌉',
            'Solo': '🎸',
            'Breakdown': '💥',
            'Outro': '◀',
            'Custom': '•'
        }
        icon = icons.get(section.type, '•')
        
        # Base text
        text = f"{icon} {section.name}"
        
        # Duration
        text += f" ({section.duration} bars)"
        
        # Key/tempo overrides
        overrides = []
        if section.key:
            overrides.append(f"Key: {section.key}")
        if section.tempo:
            overrides.append(f"{section.tempo} BPM")
        
        if overrides:
            text += f" [{', '.join(overrides)}]"
        
        return text
    
    def update_timeline(self):
        """Update timeline visualization"""
        if not self.current_project:
            self.timeline_label.setText("")
            return
        
        sections = self.current_project.master_composition.sections
        if not sections:
            self.timeline_label.setText("No sections defined")
            return
        
        # Create visual timeline
        timeline = "Timeline:\n"
        current_bar = 0
        
        for section in sections:
            # Calculate bar range
            start_bar = current_bar
            end_bar = current_bar + section.duration
            
            # Create visual bar
            bar_width = max(10, section.duration // 2)
            bar = "█" * bar_width
            
            timeline += f"{section.name:12s} [{start_bar:3d}-{end_bar:3d}] 
{bar}\n"
            
            current_bar = end_bar
        
        total_duration = sum(s.duration for s in sections)
        timeline += f"\nTotal: {total_duration} bars"
        
        self.timeline_label.setText(timeline)
    
    def on_section_selection_changed(self):
        """Handle section selection change"""
        selected_item = self.section_list.currentItem()
        has_selection = selected_item is not None
        
        # Enable/disable buttons
        self.edit_btn.setEnabled(has_selection)
        self.duplicate_btn.setEnabled(has_selection)
        self.remove_btn.setEnabled(has_selection)
        self.move_up_btn.setEnabled(has_selection and 
self.section_list.currentRow() > 0)
        self.move_down_btn.setEnabled(
            has_selection and 
            self.section_list.currentRow() < self.section_list.count() - 1
        )
        
        if has_selection:
            section_id = selected_item.data(Qt.UserRole)
            section = self.get_section_by_id(section_id)
            
            if section:
                self.selected_section = section
                self.display_section_info(section)
                self.chord_editor.load_section(section)
        else:
            self.selected_section = None
            self.section_info_label.setText("No section selected")
            self.chord_editor.clear()
    
    def display_section_info(self, section):
        """Display selected section information"""
        info = f"<b>{section.name}</b><br>"
        info += f"Type: {section.type}<br>"
        info += f"Duration: {section.duration} bars<br>"
        
        if section.key:
            info += f"Key: {section.key} (override)<br>"
        else:
            info += f"Key: {self.current_project.metadata.key} (global)<br>"
        
        if section.tempo:
            info += f"Tempo: {section.tempo} BPM (override)<br>"
        else:
            info += f"Tempo: {self.current_project.metadata.tempo} BPM 
(global)<br>"
        
        chord_count = len(section.chord_progression)
        info += f"Chords defined: {chord_count}"
        
        self.section_info_label.setText(info)
    
    def add_section(self):
        """Add new section"""
        dialog = SectionDialog(self)
        if dialog.exec():
            section_data = dialog.get_section_data()
            
            # Create section
            section = Section(
                id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
                name=section_data['name'],
                type=section_data['type'],
                duration=section_data['duration'],
                key=section_data['key'],
                tempo=section_data['tempo']
            )
            
            # Add to project
            self.current_project.add_section(section)
            
            # Refresh UI
            self.refresh_section_list()
            self.update_timeline()
            
            # Select new section
            for i in range(self.section_list.count()):
                item = self.section_list.item(i)
                if item.data(Qt.UserRole) == section.id:
                    self.section_list.setCurrentItem(item)
                    break
            
            # Emit signal
            self.section_added.emit(section)
    
    def edit_section(self):
        """Edit selected section"""
        if not self.selected_section:
            return
        
        dialog = SectionDialog(self, self.selected_section)
        if dialog.exec():
            section_data = dialog.get_section_data()
            
            # Update section
            self.selected_section.name = section_data['name']
            self.selected_section.type = section_data['type']
            self.selected_section.duration = section_data['duration']
            self.selected_section.key = section_data['key']
            self.selected_section.tempo = section_data['tempo']
            
            # Refresh UI
            self.refresh_section_list()
            self.update_timeline()
            self.display_section_info(self.selected_section)
            
            # Emit signal
            self.section_modified.emit(self.selected_section)
    
    def duplicate_section(self):
        """Duplicate selected section"""
        if not self.selected_section:
            return
        
        # Create copy
        new_section = Section(
            id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
            name=f"{self.selected_section.name} (Copy)",
            type=self.selected_section.type,
            duration=self.selected_section.duration,
            key=self.selected_section.key,
            tempo=self.selected_section.tempo,
            chord_progression=[
                ChordChange(bar=c.bar, chord=c.chord)
                for c in self.selected_section.chord_progression
            ]
        )
        
        # Add to project
        self.current_project.add_section(new_section)
        
        # Refresh UI
        self.refresh_section_list()
        self.update_timeline()
        
        # Emit signal
        self.section_added.emit(new_section)
    
    def remove_section(self):
        """Remove selected section"""
        if not self.selected_section:
            return
        
        # Confirm
        reply = QMessageBox.question(
            self,
            "Remove Section",
            f"Remove section '{self.selected_section.name}'?\n"
            f"All composition data for this section will be lost.",
            QMessageBox.Yes | QMessageBox.No,
            QMessageBox.No
        )
        
        if reply == QMessageBox.Yes:
            section_id = self.selected_section.id
            self.current_project.remove_section(section_id)
            
            # Refresh UI
            self.selected_section = None
            self.refresh_section_list()
            self.update_timeline()
            
            # Emit signal
            self.section_removed.emit(section_id)
    
    def move_section_up(self):
        """Move selected section up in order"""
        if not self.selected_section:
            return
        
        sections = self.current_project.master_composition.sections
        index = next(i for i, s in enumerate(sections) if s.id == 
self.selected_section.id)
        
        if index > 0:
            sections[index], sections[index - 1] = sections[index - 1], 
sections[index]
            self.refresh_section_list()
            self.update_timeline()
            
            # Re-select
            self.section_list.setCurrentRow(index - 1)
    
    def move_section_down(self):
        """Move selected section down in order"""
        if not self.selected_section:
            return
        
        sections = self.current_project.master_composition.sections
        index = next(i for i, s in enumerate(sections) if s.id == 
self.selected_section.id)
        
        if index < len(sections) - 1:
            sections[index], sections[index + 1] = sections[index + 1], 
sections[index]
            self.refresh_section_list()
            self.update_timeline()
            
            # Re-select
            self.section_list.setCurrentRow(index + 1)
    
    def on_chord_progression_changed(self):
        """Handle chord progression changes"""
        if self.selected_section:
            self.display_section_info(self.selected_section)
            self.section_modified.emit(self.selected_section)
    
    def get_section_by_id(self, section_id):
        """Get section by ID"""
        if not self.current_project:
            return None
        
        return next(
            (s for s in self.current_project.master_composition.sections if 
s.id == section_id),
            None
        )
```

### File 2: `ui/dialogs/section_dialog.py`

```python
"""
Sonorum - Section Dialog
Dialog for adding and editing song sections
"""

from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QDialog, QVBoxLayout, QFormLayout, QLineEdit,
    QComboBox, QSpinBox, QCheckBox, QDialogButtonBox,
    QGroupBox, QLabel
)

from core.constants import SECTION_TYPES, NOTES, MODES


class SectionDialog(QDialog):
    """Dialog for adding/editing sections"""
    
    def __init__(self, parent=None, section=None):
        super().__init__(parent)
        self.section = section
        self.is_edit_mode = section is not None
        
        # Get global defaults from parent's project
        self.parent_widget = parent
        self.global_key = None
        self.global_tempo = None
        
        if hasattr(parent, 'current_project') and parent.current_project:
            self.global_key = parent.current_project.metadata.key
            self.global_tempo = parent.current_project.metadata.tempo
        
        self.setup_ui()
        
        if self.is_edit_mode:
            self.load_section_data()
    
    def setup_ui(self):
        """Setup dialog UI"""
        self.setWindowTitle("Edit Section" if self.is_edit_mode else "Add 
Section")
        self.setModal(True)
        self.setMinimumWidth(400)
        
        layout = QVBoxLayout()
        self.setLayout(layout)
        
        # Form layout
        form = QFormLayout()
        
        # Section name
        self.name_input = QLineEdit()
        self.name_input.setPlaceholderText("e.g., Verse 1, Chorus, Bridge")
        form.addRow("Name:", self.name_input)
        
        # Section type
        self.type_combo = QComboBox()
        self.type_combo.addItems(SECTION_TYPES)
        self.type_combo.currentTextChanged.connect(self.on_type_changed)
        form.addRow("Type:", self.type_combo)
        
        # Duration (bars)
        self.duration_spin = QSpinBox()
        self.duration_spin.setRange(1, 64)
        self.duration_spin.setValue(4)
        self.duration_spin.setSuffix(" bars")
        form.addRow("Duration:", self.duration_spin)
        
        layout.addLayout(form)
        
        # Override group
        override_group = QGroupBox("Section Overrides (optional)")
        override_layout = QVBoxLayout()
        
        # Key override
        key_form = QFormLayout()
        
        self.key_override_check = QCheckBox("Override global key")
        
self.key_override_check.stateChanged.connect(self.on_key_override_changed)
        key_form.addRow(self.key_override_check)
        
        self.key_combo = QComboBox()
        self.key_combo.addItems(NOTES)
        self.key_combo.setEnabled(False)
        key_form.addRow("Key:", self.key_combo)
        
        override_layout.addLayout(key_form)
        
        # Tempo override
        tempo_form = QFormLayout()
        
        self.tempo_override_check = QCheckBox("Override global tempo")
        
self.tempo_override_check.stateChanged.connect(self.on_tempo_override_changed)
        tempo_form.addRow(self.tempo_override_check)
        
        self.tempo_spin = QSpinBox()
        self.tempo_spin.setRange(40, 300)
        self.tempo_spin.setValue(120)
        self.tempo_spin.setSuffix(" BPM")
        self.tempo_spin.setEnabled(False)
        tempo_form.addRow("Tempo:", self.tempo_spin)
        
        override_layout.addLayout(tempo_form)
        
        # Show current globals
        if self.global_key and self.global_tempo:
            global_info = QLabel(
                f"<i>Global: {self.global_key} @ {self.global_tempo} BPM</i>"
            )
            global_info.setStyleSheet("color: #888; font-size: 11px; padding: 
5px;")
            override_layout.addWidget(global_info)
        
        override_group.setLayout(override_layout)
        layout.addWidget(override_group)
        
        # Dialog buttons
        button_box = QDialogButtonBox(
            QDialogButtonBox.Ok | QDialogButtonBox.Cancel
        )
        button_box.accepted.connect(self.accept)
        button_box.rejected.connect(self.reject)
        layout.addWidget(button_box)
    
    def on_type_changed(self, type_name):
        """Handle section type change - auto-fill name if empty"""
        if not self.name_input.text() or 
self.name_input.text().startswith(self.type_combo.itemText(0)):
            # Auto-suggest name based on type
            if type_name in ['Verse', 'Chorus']:
                self.name_input.setText(f"{type_name} 1")
            else:
                self.name_input.setText(type_name)
    
    def on_key_override_changed(self, state):
        """Handle key override checkbox"""
        self.key_combo.setEnabled(state == 2)  # Qt.Checked
    
    def on_tempo_override_changed(self, state):
        """Handle tempo override checkbox"""
        self.tempo_spin.setEnabled(state == 2)
    
    def load_section_data(self):
        """Load data from existing section"""
        if not self.section:
            return
        
        self.name_input.setText(self.section.name)
        
        # Set type
        index = self.type_combo.findText(self.section.type)
        if index >= 0:
            self.type_combo.setCurrentIndex(index)
        
        self.duration_spin.setValue(self.section.duration)
        
        # Key override
        if self.section.key:
            self.key_override_check.setChecked(True)
            index = self.key_combo.findText(self.section.key)
            if index >= 0:
                self.key_combo.setCurrentIndex(index)
        
        # Tempo override
        if self.section.tempo:
            self.tempo_override_check.setChecked(True)
            self.tempo_spin.setValue(self.section.tempo)
    
    def get_section_data(self):
        """Get section data from dialog"""
        return {
            'name': self.name_input.text() or "Unnamed Section",
            'type': self.type_combo.currentText(),
            'duration': self.duration_spin.value(),
            'key': self.key_combo.currentText() if 
self.key_override_check.isChecked() else None,
            'tempo': self.tempo_spin.value() if 
self.tempo_override_check.isChecked() else None
        }
    
    def accept(self):
        """Validate and accept"""
        if not self.name_input.text().strip():
            self.name_input.setFocus()
            return
        
        super().accept()
```
